# W11 final scored run: DeepSeek, map-reduce, hybrid-norerank retrieval (#250)

**Blind full read: 21/30 (tier 1: 12/18).** Same 30 plans as the fts run on `main` (`docs/results/w11-report.md`, blind full read 19/30, tier 1 11/18). Second read and adjudicated score: PR review round 1. One sample per question.

Against: W10 Claude hybrid-norerank #240 25/30; #243 DeepSeek baseline 15/30; #247's adjudicated eight 6/8.

## How this was run

- Code: `exp/hybrid` 36b0b58 (merge of PR #266, contains main 08b0ac4), run from `tl/250-hybrid` at 314973d. Plan: `plans/0250-final-runs.md` (approved at d5f337d with TL conditions C1-C4).
- Snapshot `corpus-2026-11-19`, as-of 2026-11-17, Q1-Q30. Model `deepseek-v4-pro` on `https://api.deepseek.com/beta`, thinking on, request `ds-strict-1-think-on-a64000-p64000`, planner `plan-v3.1`, extract `extract-v1.1`, reduce `v5.2`, `SUBQUESTION_TOKENS=40000`, `MAP_CONCURRENCY=6`. Settings pinned in `run-scored.ps1` / `.sh`; `.env` copied in and removed, never read.
- Plans: all 30 from the fts run's saved planner records (`--reuse-plans deepseek:deepseek-v4-pro`), 0 planner calls; plans identical in both modes (30/30).
- Run: `--max-calls 177` (from the 0-call count: round-1 maps + one re-extraction per row key + one reduce per question), one attempt, exit 0, **118 new calls**, 30 reused. No ProviderStop, no 400, no cap reached. 30 records reused (the plans). No map / remap record was inherited: every round-1 map input differs from the fts run and #247 (`count-hyb.md`); no fts reduce matched.
- Cost of this run: $7.84 new; $8.29 for every call the 30 answers used (input 3,860,914 tokens, 220,160 cache hits; output 2,934,294, of which reasoning 2,551,617); 140 calls off-peak, 8 peak (prices `config/pricing.yaml`, by completion time).
- Replay (0 calls, the run's `raw/` as cache): every answer identical except Q12 (see Findings).
- Scoring: blind, inputs `ev250\blind-in-hyb\` only (dump split in two files, Q1-Q15 / Q16-Q30, read in full). The dump was too large for one scorer: four Sonnet scorers each took a range (Q1-8, Q9-15, Q16-21, Q22-30) under the same prompt; part files and the merged file (sha256 `b1c85d3468441d0c...`) committed alone in b3b4ebb before any comparison. The hybrid scorers never saw the fts answers or verdicts.

## Changes against #243, W10 and #247

Every pipeline element changed at once between these runs and W10 (Claude, single answer call, `plan-v2`) and #243 (DeepSeek, single answer call, `plan-v2`): this run has DeepSeek `plan-v3.1` sub-questions (firm x aspect), one extraction call per firm, corrective re-search and a reduce call. So a change is labelled **pipeline + plan** (and **model** against W10) unless the scorer reasons name a narrower cause; the reasons of both reads are quoted. #247's eight: Q1, Q3, Q11, Q19, Q22, Q23, Q24, Q26 (tuned on in #247).

| Q | Change | This run (reason) |
|---|---|---|
| Q3 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Advisor Services framing, priorities (support ecosystem, banking/lending), competitors named, and concessions (wirehouse banking, long/short throttled, referral network cut). |
| Q5 | W10 hybrid PASS -> FAIL | Only one named advisor platform (LeadIQ) plus generic E*TRADE/AI quotes from 32 passages; thin versus the ask for platforms with stated purpose; no spend invented. |
| Q7 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Same dimensions across all three with visible gaps (MS training, MS recent headcount, MER payout/succession); borderline for stale 2020 MS headcount and trade-press cells. |
| Q8 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Each firm's own language, custody (Schwab) and affiliation (RJF) kept distinct in claims; borderline because the comparison claim does not explicitly name the business-model difference. |
| Q12 | W10 hybrid PASS -> FAIL | Schwab acquisition evidence is good (40% marketing-driven, cost per account, account growth) and replication is handed off, but Edward Jones's own disclosed cost structure is not set alongside (only footprint and initiatives); borderline. |
| Q15 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Explains mechanism (NII on client cash plus fees and trading vs asset-based advisory fees, MS margin driver, sweep economics) from filings, says the quantitative comparison is invalid and EDJ lacks a driver statement; Schwab has no disclosed revenue split and the cash-yield mechanism leans on tier-4 trade press; borderline. |
| Q16 | #243 PASS -> FAIL; W10 hybrid PASS -> FAIL | Dump is a flat dated claim list (71 claims, firm statements interleaved with research, some off-topic such as JPM privacy and MS channel descriptions); not organised by position, no contrasting spread; no own forecast, attributed and dated. |
| Q17 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Claims split into stated plans/aspirations (a2), named deployed tools (a3) and reported outcomes/usage (a4), so specificity is distinguished from volume; declines to rank 'most' as not valid on non-common basis, which passes_if does not require. |
| Q18 | #243 PASS -> FAIL; W10 hybrid PASS -> FAIL | Refused (comparison_missing) where answer was expected, although the corpus held fee passages (McKinsey fee stabilisation, Cerulli fee-based preference, ADV fee schedules); no labelled distinction of disclosed changes, regulation, research and commentary. |
| Q19 | #243 FAIL -> PASS; W10 hybrid FAIL -> PASS | Dated sourced cohort evidence (BofA PB, FINRA, Schwab, EDJ 10-K) then explicit stop at 'should' (C44, C45) handing back to strategy; competitor moves thin (Schwab only, MS/MER/RJF gaps). |
| Q20 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Real survey findings with source, date and sample (UBS, FINRA under-35, BofA 21-45, Wealthtender 35-64 with n=500); but no disagreement between surveys surfaced, Wealthtender is not under 40, and some UBS claims are succession-planning rather than advisor choice. |
| Q21 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | Two halves kept separate: Cerulli $123.7T to 2048 (Oct 2025) and UBS $83T (Apr 2026) labelled by source and date, versus per-firm statements (Schwab, EDJ, RJF, BAC) with honest gaps for MS/Merrill; cluttered with headcount/NNA filler and C6 $85T citation shows unrelated opening text. |
| Q24 | #243 FAIL -> PASS; W10 hybrid FAIL -> PASS | Declines the bad comparison and names per-firm why it is invalid (different numerators and advisor definitions, RJF's own caveat) with citations; borderline because it computes no figure even for Edward Jones where both inputs are disclosed on one basis. |
| Q25 | #243 PASS -> FAIL; W10 hybrid PASS -> FAIL | Relies on trade-press (InvestmentNews) claims on MS pay threshold, retention bonuses and clawbacks as if disclosed, contrary to passes_if; also pads with ADV/10-K items and the undisclosed rest is only partly marked. |
| Q26 | #243 FAIL -> PASS; W10 hybrid FAIL -> PASS | All items fall in the 2026-08-19 to 11-17 window with dates and cited docs; borderline because every item is tier-4 trade press, the 'why it matters' lines mostly restate other facts, and ordering/links are not clearly newest-first. |
| Q27 | #243 PASS -> FAIL; W10 hybrid PASS -> FAIL | Incomplete answer: every Merrill sub-question matrix_failed (model output invalid), answerable=False, nothing concluded; expected answer. |
| Q28 | #243 FAIL -> PASS | EDJ rate (6.1% 3Q25, 6.4% 2Q25) with period, own definition and 10-Q cites; Merrill only qualitative 'near historic lows'; no valid comparison stated; headcount given as adjacent and labelled not attrition, though net headcount change is not computed and industry research is added. |
| Q29 | #243 PASS -> FAIL; W10 hybrid PASS -> FAIL | Got 'answer' not gap_with_adjacent: never states payout grids are not public, names no source that would answer it, and cites InvestmentNews pay-threshold claim without an indicative-only caveat; aggregate WM comp expense is labelled a different quantity but comes from 10-Q/supplement rather than the 10-K. |

| Q28 | FAIL bl | PASS bl | 137 / 149 (113) | 16 / 15 (14) | scorer reading / wording: same EDJ rate and Merrill qualitative evidence in both (14 of 16 / 15 cited chunks shared); the scorers differ on whether net headcount vs attrition is stated plainly |

**Q19 and the ADR-018 competitor floor** (TL decisions on #250, issuecomment-7047676757 and the a1.RJF extension): on the DeepSeek plans the floor fires only in Q19: fts drops 4 of 5 Merrill chunks (a1.MER) and the single RJF chunk (a1.RJF, emptied); hybrid-norerank drops none (`mock-real-plans.md`). Any Q19 difference that comes from Merrill or RJF competitor passages is attributed to the floor, not to ranking. Q19: fts PASS bl, hybrid PASS bl.

Map records shared between the modes: none (every map input differs). Reduces shared: none.

- **Q10** fts: Expected answer but got gap_with_adjacent: declines to name or rank any firm as most explicit, so no judgment is made legible, although dated statements per firm are shown.
  hybrid: Shows dated statements for all five firms and states the basis for naming Merrill (only firm naming a 'Mass Affluent' tier) while labelling Schwab/RJF/MS/EDJ statements as adjacent; reader can disagree.

- **Q12** fts: Full Schwab evidence (scale quotes, cost per account, ~40% marketing-driven, ad spend, accounts, workplace) and an explicit normative hand-off; borderline because Edward Jones's own cost structure is thin (no expense figures set alongside).
  hybrid: Schwab acquisition evidence is good (40% marketing-driven, cost per account, account growth) and replication is handed off, but Edward Jones's own disclosed cost structure is not set alongside (only footprint and initiatives); borderline.

- **Q20** fts: Incomplete output: every sub-question matrix_failed (model output invalid/call failed), so no survey findings were returned.
  hybrid: Real survey findings with source, date and sample (UBS, FINRA under-35, BofA 21-45, Wealthtender 35-64 with n=500); but no disagreement between surveys surfaced, Wealthtender is not under 40, and some UBS claims are succession-planning rather than advisor choice.

- **Q21** fts: Invalid output: no answer record (model none, reduce invalid), nothing delivered.
  hybrid: Two halves kept separate: Cerulli $123.7T to 2048 (Oct 2025) and UBS $83T (Apr 2026) labelled by source and date, versus per-firm statements (Schwab, EDJ, RJF, BAC) with honest gaps for MS/Merrill; cluttered with headcount/NNA filler and C6 $85T citation shows unrelated opening text.

- **Q27** fts: Merrill-specific items (trade press, 9/15 press release, Merrill Wealth Management lines) with BAC GWIM material labelled as GWIM-not-Merrill and gaps declared for headcount/NNA; C1/C2 co-president and regional-head items date from 2023 and are not changes this year.
  hybrid: Incomplete answer: every Merrill sub-question matrix_failed (model output invalid), answerable=False, nothing concluded; expected answer.

- **Q28** fts: EDJ rate (6.1% 3Q25, 6.4% 2Q25) with definition and 10-Q cite, and Merrill 'near historic lows' qualitative only, are correct; but the adjacent net headcount change and its difference from attrition is never stated plainly (only raw headcount points, Merrill's stale 2023).
  hybrid: EDJ rate (6.1% 3Q25, 6.4% 2Q25) with period, own definition and 10-Q cites; Merrill only qualitative 'near historic lows'; no valid comparison stated; headcount given as adjacent and labelled not attrition, though net headcount change is not computed and industry research is added.

## Cost

Calls used by the 30 answers: 148 ({'plan': 30, 'map': 76, 'remap': 14, 'reduce': 28, 'answer': 0}), 118 new in this run, 30 reused. USD new $7.84, all calls $8.29, all at peak price $16.44. Mean per question: $0.276 (all calls), $0.261 (new). Per question: the Scores table.

#250 in total: 22 planner + 79 fts + 118 hybrid = 219 new calls, about $14.73.

## Findings

- Q27: `map.MER` was cut at max_tokens (64,000 output tokens): the question was answered without the Merrill cell; kept, not re-called (TL ruling).
- Q12: the 0-call replay of the run (same saved map records) built a different code caveat for Q12 (series span '2024 to 2026Q3' in the run, '2024Q4 to 2026Q3' in the replay), so the replay's reduce key did not match and its answer was not reused. The scored record is the run's own answer (TL ruling). Follow-up in NEXT: a reduce caveat the code writes from saved map outputs is not deterministic.

### Q2 (answer)

- C1 On the earnings call for the period ended December 31, 2025 (published January 28, 2026), CEO Paul Shoukry said Raymond James' optimism about future growth is fueled by its robust advisor recruiting pipeline, and that its unique combination of an advisor- and client-focused culture with leading technology and solutions, strong balance sheet, and commitment to independence is proving to be a differentiator for advisors evaluating alternatives: "We offer a unique combination of an advisor and client-focused culture, coupled with leading technology and solutions. This value proposition, coupled with our strong balance sheet and commitment to independence, is proving to be a differentiator for advisors evaluating alternatives." [RJF-TR-2025Q4#5]
- C2 The fiscal 2025 10-K (period ended September 30, 2025) states continuous recruitment and retention of financial advisors is an important driver of success, and that the firm's ability to attract high-quality advisors is based on its values-based culture, commitment to service, and unique ways it provides services to financial advisors: "Our ability to attract high quality advisors is based on our values-based culture, our commitment to service, and the unique ways in which we provide services to our financial advisors." - C77 On this evidence, the contrast Raymond James draws against wirehouses for advisors can be made, as Raymond James' own stated positioning. On the earnings call for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published October 22, 2025), CEO Paul Shoukry contrasted its approach: it attracts and retains advisors with unique culture, leading service, and robust platform, whereas many competitors compete with the largest check, and it values independence whereas many competitors change comp plans every year to cross-sell bank products: "We attract and retain financial advisors with our unique culture, leading service, and robust platform, whereas many of our competitors compete with the largest check. We value independence to foster an environment where our advisors can provide objective advice to their clients, whereas many of our competitors change their comp plans every year to cross-sell more bank products." On the earnings call for the period ended June 30, 2026 (published July 22, 2026), he said Raymond James has the largest addressable market because it offers all affiliation options and is agnostic about how advisors affiliate, and that very few firms treat advisers like clients anymore. On the earnings call for the period ended December 31, 2025 (published January 28, 2026), he said competitors with similar cultures have technology investment that is a fraction of Raymond James' roughly $1 billion, making it hard for them to remain competitive on AI and tools: "a lot of our competitors who also are focused on personal relationships, and that have similar cultures, their technology investment, for example, is a fraction of ours." On the earnings call for the period ended March 31, 2025 (published April 23, 2025), he said Raymond James wants to use AI to empower advisors to better serve clients, whereas many competitors have used technology and plan to use AI to go around advisors and directly to clients. This is a qualitative self-characterization, not a like-for-like numeric comparison across firms. [RJF-TR-2025Q3#26, RJF-TR-2026Q2#26, RJF-TR-2025Q4#34, RJF-TR-2025Q1#79]

### Q3 (answer)

- C1 The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026) states that, through its Advisor Services segment, Schwab has become one of the largest providers of custodial, trading, banking, and support services to RIAs and their clients, and that management believes Schwab can maintain its competitive position primarily through its sales, support, technology, and business consulting teams, which are dedicated to helping RIAs grow, compete, and succeed in serving their clients. [SCHW-10K-2025#23]
- C2 On the 3Q26 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), Schwab's CEO stated that Schwab has built its advisor business around helping independent advisors grow, compete, and succeed. [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#10]
- C3 On the 3Q25 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025), Schwab stated it is deepening relationships with the 16,000 RIA firms it serves by building a broader support ecosystem that extends beyond custody alone to add value for RIAs, including lending support, alternatives capability, and an expanded institutional no-transaction-fee mutual fund platform. [SCHW-TR-2025Q3#13]
- C4 Trade press reports (published October 28, 2026) that Schwab's CEO stated Schwab will continue to focus on its custody services for RIAs with under $100 million AUM and that Schwab's role is to provide a platform and a support ecosystem that helps RIAs support their clients and helps their firms thrive. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-28-WM-01#0]
- C5 Trade press reports (published August 26, 2026) that a Schwab spokesperson stated Schwab, as the largest custodian for advisors, is focused on creating long-term value and access for advisors and retail investors and on investing in innovation, capabilities, and service to help advisors grow. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-08-26-WM-01#1]
- C6 The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026) states that the Advisor Services segment provides custodial, trading, banking and trust, and support services to independent registered investment advisors (RIAs), independent retirement advisors, and recordkeepers. [SCHW-10K-2025#11]
- C7 The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026) states that RIAs and their clients have access to Schwab's broad range of wealth services and that, by functioning as the custodian, Schwab earns revenue associated with the underlying client assets, predominantly through net interest revenue and asset management and administration fees, and does not charge the RIA or end client a custody fee. [SCHW-10K-2025#26]
- C8 On the 3Q26 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), Schwab stated it serves more than 16,000 firms representing $5.8 trillion in assets with a platform that brings together custody, trading, banking and lending capabilities, technology, and dedicated service and support. [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#10]
- C9 In its May 14, 2026 investor day deck, Schwab states that Advisor Services is in a position of strength and remains a driving force for Schwab, with $5.2T in client assets, 16K advisory firms, and 3.9M households, and is #1 in RIA custodial assets. [SCHW-DECK-2026-investor-day#62]
- C10 In its May 14, 2026 investor day deck, Schwab states that Schwab Advisor Services provides custody, trading, and the support services of Charles Schwab & Co., Inc. to independent investment advisors. [SCHW-DECK-2026-investor-day#307]
- C11 In its May 14, 2026 investor day deck, Schwab states its strategic priority is to be the leading RIA support ecosystem to help advisors serve their clients, providing wealth tools, investment/lending products, and technology in addition to custody and service. [SCHW-DECK-2026-investor-day#35]
- C12 On the 1Q26 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2026), Schwab stated that in Advisor Services it is continuing to enhance its digital experiences across RIA workflows such as move money, account open, and account maintenance while also modernizing tools on its advisor platform. [SCHW-TR-2026Q1#8]
- C13 On the 4Q24 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2024), Schwab stated that in advisor services it is adding support and capabilities for RIAs of all sizes while also helping new advisors transition to independence. [SCHW-TR-2024Q4#13]
- C14 On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), Schwab stated it is investing in banking and lending for RIAs as the natural provider of those capabilities and that it is just getting started with its banking journey for RIAs. [SCHW-TR-2025Q4#48]
- C15 On the 2Q25 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2025), Schwab stated that in its advisor services business it launched Advisor ProDirect, a fee-based, membership-driven offer designed to support independence for new RIAs. [SCHW-TR-2025Q2#11]
- C16 The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026) states that, in the Advisor Services arena, Schwab competes with institutional custodians, wirehouses, regional and independent broker-dealers, fintech custodians, banks, and trust companies. [SCHW-10K-2025#13]
- C17 On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), Schwab's CEO stated that RIAs from time to time compete against the wealth divisions of wirehouses and that wirehouses are doing a better job of leveraging their banking capabilities. [SCHW-TR-2025Q4#48]
- C18 Trade press reports (published October 29, 2026) that Schwab's CEO stated independent advisors are competing against wirehouses that typically have really strong banks attached to their advice. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-29-WM-01#0]
- C19 On the 3Q26 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), Schwab's CEO stated that Schwab has changed the minimums for long/short strategies and throttled growth to some extent. [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#42]
- C20 Trade press reports (published October 28, 2026) that Schwab stated it limited long-short offerings to clients with at least $10 million in assets, curtailing their use by many advisors. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-28-WM-01#1]
- C21 Trade press reports (published October 28, 2026) that Schwab stated it is reducing the Schwab Advisor Network referral network by raising the bar for participation to only clients with over $5 million and RIAs with at least $500 million in AUM. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-28-WM-01#1]
- C22 Trade press reports (published August 24, 2026) that a Schwab spokesperson confirmed the asset minimum for client referrals in Schwab Advisor Network has changed from $2 million to $5 million, effective January 5, 2027. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-08-24-IN-01#0]

### Q5 (answer)

- C1 Morgan Stanley: On the 4Q24 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2024; published January 16, 2025), management said it has been investing in its Wealth Management processes and systems "across the technology side, across better understanding our data, better servicing our clients in terms of investment dollars and in terms of the underlying infrastructure." [MS-TR-2024Q4#44]
- C2 Morgan Stanley: On the 1Q25 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2025; published April 11, 2025), management said it is seeing the benefits of investments it has been making into its advisor-led platform over multiple years: "we are seeing the benefits of the investments that we have been making into that platform over multiple years, including even the most recent last year." [MS-TR-2025Q1#27]
- C3 Morgan Stanley: On the 1Q25 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2025; published April 11, 2025), management said its financial advisors can offer clients world-class technology through the integrated firm: "the financial advisor is seeing the integrated firm for what it is, which is we can offer unique access to intellectual capital, world-class technology". [MS-TR-2025Q1#54]
- C4 Morgan Stanley: On the 2Q25 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2025; published July 16, 2025), management said its ongoing investments in its world-class platform include investments to support its advisors, a build-out of E*TRADE capabilities, and expansion of its workplace channel: "Ongoing investments in our world-class platform, including investments to support our advisors, a build-out of E*TRADE capabilities, and the expansion of our essential workplace channel, serve as an engine for future growth." [MS-TR-2025Q2#3]
- C5 Morgan Stanley: On the 3Q25 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025; published October 15, 2025), management described LeadIQ, its AI-powered lead distribution platform, as an early AI use case that matches workplace and self-directed relationships and facilitates engagement with its financial advisors: "LeadIQ, our AI-powered lead distribution platform, focusing on matching workplace and self-directed relationships and facilitating engagement with our financial advisors." [MS-TR-2025Q3#8]
- C6 Morgan Stanley: On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025; published January 15, 2026), management said it continues to invest in its Wealth segment via technology, and that its growth plans embed increasing adoption of AI tools throughout the enterprise and inside its client base: "We continue to invest in each of our three business segments: Wealth, Institutional Securities, and Investment Management via human capital and technology. Our growth plans embed the increasing adoption of AI tools throughout the enterprise and inside our client base." [MS-TR-2025Q4#8]
- C7 Morgan Stanley: On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025; published January 15, 2026), management said LeadIQ is an AI tool used on the revenue side that helps introduce its advisors to clients interested in advice: "LeadIQ, for example, which is helping us introduce our advisors to our clients who are interested in advice, that's happening using AI." [MS-TR-2025Q4#35]
- C8 Morgan Stanley: In its FY2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025; published February 19, 2026), the company said competition in its Wealth Management segment is affected by its technology supporting evolving client needs, including generative artificial intelligence and tokenization: "our technology supporting evolving client needs, including generative artificial intelligence and tokenization." [MS-10K-2025#25]

### Q6 (answer)

- C1 Raymond James Financial: In its Form 10-K for the fiscal year ended September 30, 2025 (Item 1, published November 25, 2025), Raymond James states that it offers multiple affiliation options, which it refers to as AdvisorChoice®, and that financial advisors primarily affiliate directly as employees or independent contractors, or as employees of third-party Registered Investment Advisors (“RIAs”) and broker-dealers served through its RIA and Custody Services (“RCS”) division: "We offer multiple affiliation options, which we refer to as AdvisorChoice®. Financial advisors primarily affiliate with us directly as either employees or independent contractors or as employees of third-party Registered Investment Advisors (“RIAs”) and broker-dealers to which we provide services through our RIA and Custody Services (“RCS”) division." [RJF-10K-2025#10]
- C2 Raymond James Financial: In its Form 10-K for the fiscal year ended September 30, 2025 (Item 1, published November 25, 2025), Raymond James states that independent contractor financial advisors are generally responsible for all of their direct costs and therefore receive a higher payout percentage on the revenues they generate than employee financial advisors, and that independent contractor advisors may affiliate directly or through an affiliated bank or credit union in its Financial Institutions Division: "Our financial advisors who are independent contractors are generally responsible for all of their direct costs and, accordingly, receive a higher payout percentage on the revenues they generate than employee financial advisors. Our independent contractor financial advisor options are designed to help our advisors build their businesses with as much or as little of our support as they determine they need. Independent contractor financial advisors may affiliate with us directly or through an affiliated bank or credit union in our Financial Institutions Division." [RJF-10K-2025#11]
- C3 Raymond James Financial: On its affiliation options web page (content as of April 21, 2026), Raymond James says that through AdvisorChoice® advisors can match their professional goals to the affiliation option that is right for them, and the page lists the options as Traditional Employee, Advisor Select, Independent Contractor, Bank & Credit Union, and Corporate RIA, each offering a unique combination of support, freedom, and flexibility: "Through AdvisorChoice®, you have the ability to match your professional goals to the affiliation option that’s right for you, each offering a unique combination of support, freedom and flexibility – and all backed by the full support of a leading independent financial firm." [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#0]
- C4 Raymond James Financial: In its 2025 Investor Day deck (published June 5, 2025), Raymond James presents the AdvisorChoice spectrum as spanning employee advisor (RJA), Advisor Select, independent advisor (ICD), banks and credit unions, IAR (corporate RIA), and RIA & custody services, and states: "The movement to 'independence' is not channel-specific." [RJF-DECK-2025-investor-day#38]
- C5 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended December 31, 2025 (published January 28, 2026), Raymond James said it has the largest addressable market across its affiliation options—from employee to independent contractor to the RIA custody—with critical mass and decades of experience in all of them: "we have the largest addressable market across our affiliation options, from employee to independent contractor to the RIA custody, and we have critical mass and decades of experience in all of them." [RJF-TR-2025Q4#50]
- C6 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended September 30, 2025 (published October 22, 2025), management said recruiting success has been broad-based across all of the firm's affiliation options—the employee, independent contractor, and RIA custody channels: "The recruiting success that we've been having has really been broad-based across all of our affiliation options, the employee, independent contractor, and the RAA custody channels." [RJF-TR-2025Q3#30]
- C7 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the period ended March 31, 2026 (published April 22, 2026), the CEO said the firm is seeing a pretty good uptick in the employee affiliation option as well, and that the mix shift he referred to relates to the comp ratio because the independent channel has a higher payout than the employee channel: "we're actually seeing pretty good uptick in the employee affiliation option as well. And the mix shift is really what I was referring to more about the comp ratio than the margin because the payout difference in the independent channel versus the employee channel." [RJF-TR-2026Q1#46]
- C8 Raymond James Financial: On the earnings call for the fiscal third quarter of 2025 (period ended June 30, 2025, published July 23, 2025), the CEO said the firm was seeing good success across all of its affiliation options, with the acceleration rate within the independent channel probably higher, while the employee affiliation option and the independent RIA option also saw very good success: "I would say we are seeing good success across all of our affiliation options. Certainly, the acceleration rate within the independent channel is probably higher, but we are still seeing very good success in the employee affiliation option and the independent RIA, option as well." [RJF-TR-2025Q2#66]
- C9 Raymond James Financial: Whether the mix across the affiliation models has shifted can be compared on this evidence only for the employee and independent contractor affiliation options and only at the three period-ends of September 30, 2023, September 30, 2024, and September 30, 2025. The 10-K reports employee, independent contractor, and total financial advisor headcounts on the same basis for those same three period-ends, and states that advisors in the RCS division are not included in the financial advisor metric. The available figures are headcounts—a stand-in for the mix—and no separate headcounts for the other AdvisorChoice options or any figure after September 30, 2025 were found, so the full AdvisorChoice mix cannot be compared on this evidence. [RJF-10K-2025#227, RJF-10K-2025#228]

- A2 Raymond James Financial: As of September 30, 2025, Raymond James reports 5,065 independent contractor financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227, RJF-10K-2025#228]
- A3 Raymond James Financial: As of September 30, 2025, Raymond James reports 8,943 total financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227, RJF-10K-2025#228]
- A4 Raymond James Financial: As of September 30, 2024, Raymond James reports 3,826 employee financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227, RJF-10K-2025#228]
- A5 Raymond James Financial: As of September 30, 2024, Raymond James reports 4,961 independent contractor financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227, RJF-10K-2025#228]
- A6 Raymond James Financial: As of September 30, 2024, Raymond James reports 8,787 total financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227, RJF-10K-2025#228]
- A7 Raymond James Financial: As of September 30, 2023, Raymond James reports 3,693 employee financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227, RJF-10K-2025#228]
- A8 Raymond James Financial: As of September 30, 2023, Raymond James reports 5,019 independent contractor financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227, RJF-10K-2025#228]
- A9 Raymond James Financial: As of September 30, 2023, Raymond James reports 8,712 total financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#227, RJF-10K-2025#228]

### Q7 (answer)

### Q8 (answer)

- C1 Schwab: On Schwab's third-quarter 2026 earnings call (October 2026), Schwab's CEO said the firm has built its advisor business around helping independent advisors grow, compete and succeed, with a platform that brings together custody, trading, banking and lending capabilities, technology, and dedicated service and support, and said Schwab serves more than 16,000 firms representing $5.8 trillion in assets. [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#10]
- C2 Schwab: On Schwab's third-quarter 2026 earnings call (October 2026), Schwab's CEO said the advisor business requires scale, trust, operational excellence, outstanding service, and the breadth to support advisors as their businesses and clients' needs become more complex, and said that is why Schwab wins. [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#10]
- C3 Schwab: Schwab's 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that its Advisor Services segment provides custodial, trading, banking and trust, and support services to independent registered investment advisors (RIAs), independent retirement advisors, and recordkeepers. [SCHW-10K-2025#11]
- C4 Schwab: On Schwab's fourth-quarter 2025 earnings call (January 2026), Schwab's CEO said it is hard for other firms to compete with Schwab's size, scale, and the breadth and strength of its capabilities in Advisor Services, including the custody offering, consulting and advice, and support for advisors' drive to independence. [SCHW-TR-2025Q4#45]
- C5 Schwab: On Schwab's third-quarter 2025 earnings call (October 2025), Schwab's CEO said the firm is deepening relationships with the 16,000 RIA firms it serves by building a broader support ecosystem that extends beyond custody alone to add value for RIAs, including lending support, alternatives capability, and an expanded institutional no-transaction-fee mutual fund platform. [SCHW-TR-2025Q3#13]
- C6 Schwab: In Schwab's May 2026 investor day deck, Schwab describes itself as the leading RIA support ecosystem to help advisors serve their clients, providing wealth tools, investment/lending products, and technology in addition to custody and service. [SCHW-DECK-2026-investor-day#35]
- C7 Schwab: On Schwab's first-quarter 2026 earnings call (April 2026), Schwab's CEO said independent advisors win in the marketplace because the fiduciary model works and there is a bull market for advice and convenience, and that as advisors win and Schwab supports their growth, Schwab wins as well. [SCHW-TR-2026Q1#49]
- C8 Schwab: On Schwab's fourth-quarter 2024 earnings call (January 2025), Schwab's CEO said that in advisor services the firm is adding support and capabilities for RIAs of all sizes while also helping new advisors transition to independence. [SCHW-TR-2024Q4#13]
- C9 Schwab: On Schwab's second-quarter 2025 earnings call (July 2025), Schwab's CEO said the firm launched Advisor ProDirect, a fee-based, membership-driven offer designed to support independence for new RIAs. [SCHW-TR-2025Q2#11]
- C10 Schwab: Trade press reports (October 29, 2026) that Schwab's CEO said Schwab wants to give registered investment advisors equal or better access than wirehouse counterparts to banking services such as lending, private market investing, and long-short investment strategies, and is increasingly trying to play the role of being the advisors' bank. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-10-29-WM-01#0]
- C11 Schwab: Trade press reports (October 28, 2026) that Schwab's CEO said the firm's job and role is to provide a platform and a support ecosystem that helps advisors support their clients and helps their firm thrive, not to tell advisors what they should become or how big they should be. Raymond James emphasizes its best-of-both-worlds combination of an advisor- and client-focused culture coupled with leading technology and solutions, offered through multiple affiliation options under AdvisorChoice, and says it leads with culture and capabilities rather than the highest check, contrasting itself with firms that talk about IRRs and exit periods. Schwab's cited materials under this aspect contain no comparable explicit contrast with competitors, while Raymond James's cited materials include explicit contrasts with other firms. - C27 Raymond James: On Raymond James's earnings call for the period ended December 31, 2025 (January 2026), Paul Shoukry said many competitors that also focus on personal relationships and have similar cultures invest a fraction of Raymond James's technology spend, while RJF's long-term focus and personal relationships differentiate it. [RJF-TR-2025Q2#40]

[RJF-TR-2026Q2#6]

### Q9 (answer)

- C1 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 Form 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) describes Wealth Management as follows: "Wealth Management provides a comprehensive array of financial services and solutions to individual investors, including high and ultra-high net worth individuals, and businesses and institutions. Wealth Management supports clients through three channels: Advisor-Led, Self-Directed and Workplace." The first-quarter 2026 Form 10-Q (period ended March 31, 2026) repeats the same description. [MS-10K-2025#125, MS-10Q-2026Q1#16]
- C2 Merrill: The 2025 Form 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that Merrill Wealth Management's advisory business provides a high-touch client experience through a network of financial advisors focused on clients with over $250,000 in total investable assets, and that Bank of America Private Bank, together with Merrill Wealth Management's Private Wealth Management business, provides comprehensive wealth management solutions targeted to high net worth and ultra high net worth clients. In its November 5, 2025 investor-day deck, Merrill | Private Bank describes its approach as "Serving the wealth continuum of clients," and the firm says the Wealth Continuum allows it to deliver as one company, is its engine for lasting growth, and gives it the capabilities to deliver for every client in the way they want to be served. [BAC-10K-2025#246, BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#9, BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#8]
- C3 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 Form 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) labels high net worth and ultra-high net worth individuals as Wealth Management client categories, and labels three Wealth Management client channels: Advisor-Led, Self-Directed and Workplace. The same Form 10-K describes U.S. Bank Subsidiaries customers as including high to ultra-high net worth individuals, and separately references ultra-high net worth clients as the target of tailored lending in the Wealth Management business segment. [MS-10K-2025#125, MS-10K-2025#190]
- C4 Merrill: In its November 5, 2025 investor-day deck, Merrill's Wealth Continuum uses the labels "Mass Affluent" and "High Net Worth" for client tiers. The first-quarter 2026 deck (period ended March 31, 2026) uses the label "Merrill primary households" for households with $250K+ in investable assets within the enterprise, excluding Stock Plan and Banking-only households, and the label "Private Bank core relationships" for relationships with $3MM+ in total balances. The 2025 Form 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) uses the labels "high net worth and ultra high net worth clients" for the Private Bank and Merrill Private Wealth Management business. [BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#12, BAC-DECK-2026Q1#27, BAC-10K-2025#246]
- C5 Merrill: The 2025 Form 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that Merrill Wealth Management's advisory business is focused on clients with over $250,000 in total investable assets. The November 5, 2025 investor-day deck defines the Wealth Continuum's Mass Affluent tier as $500K - $1MM and its High Net Worth tier as $1MM - $10MM, and reports 54K $10MM+ households, indicating a $10MM+ household segment. The first-quarter 2026 deck (period ended March 31, 2026) defines Merrill primary households as those with $250K+ in investable assets within the enterprise, excluding stock plan and banking-only households, and defines Bank of America Private Bank core relationships as those with $3MM+ in total balances. On the third-quarter 2026 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), management said Merrill added about 4,200 net new households with at least $500,000 in assets and the Private Bank added 470 net new relationships with at least $3 million under management. [BAC-10K-2025#246, BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#12, BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#18, BAC-DECK-2026Q1#27, BAC-TR-2026Q3#19]
- C6 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 Form 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that lending activity in the U.S. Bank Subsidiaries from the Wealth Management business segment primarily includes securities-based lending, other forms of secured loans including tailored lending to ultra-high net worth clients, and residential real estate loans. On the third-quarter 2026 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), the CEO said Morgan Stanley clients, importantly including ultra-high net worth clients, have access to private markets via equities, co-investment opportunities, and family office infrastructure solutions. The second-quarter 2026 Form 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2026) states that Wealth Management commercial real estate loans are originated through the private banking platform and generally benefit from full or partial guarantees from high or ultra-high net worth clients. [MS-10K-2025#190, MS-TR-2026Q3#33, MS-10Q-2026Q2#186]
- C7 Merrill: The 2025 Form 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that Merrill Wealth Management's advisory business provides a high-touch client experience through a network of financial advisors, and provides tailored solutions through a full set of investment management, brokerage, banking and retirement products. It also states that for high net worth and ultra high net worth clients, Bank of America Private Bank and Merrill Private Wealth Management provide comprehensive wealth management and customized solutions for wealth structuring, investment management, trust and banking needs, including specialty asset management services. An undated Merrill web page (page content as of November 3, 2026) states Merrill provides robust solutions for ultra-high-net-worth clients, including alternative investments and sustainable and impact investing strategies. [BAC-10K-2025#246, MER-PR-web-becoming-a-merrill-lynch-advisor#0]
- C8 Morgan Stanley and Merrill: The comparison of client segmentation approaches by wealth tier is valid only at the level of stated approach and labels, not at the level of wealth thresholds. Morgan Stanley's 2025 Form 10-K describes Wealth Management as serving individual investors including high and ultra-high net worth individuals and segments clients through three channels (Advisor-Led, Self-Directed, Workplace), without stating wealth thresholds in the documents searched. Merrill's 2025 Form 10-K and investor-day/deck materials segment clients through a "wealth continuum" with dollar-denominated tiers and qualifying segments—Mass Affluent ($500K - $1MM), High Net Worth ($1MM - $10MM), Merrill primary households ($250K+ investable assets within the enterprise), and Private Bank core relationships ($3MM+ total balances). The threshold-level comparison is not valid because Morgan Stanley states no comparable wealth thresholds, and Merrill's thresholds use different units and scopes. [MS-10K-2025#125, BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#9, BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#12, BAC-DECK-2026Q1#27, BAC-10K-2025#246]
- G Morgan Stanley - The wealth thresholds or minimum investable assets that define each tier or client segment: not found in the documents searched.

### Q10 (answer)

[BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#12]

[BAC-10K-2025#246]
- C4 Merrill: In Global Wealth & Investment Management investor-day materials (published November 5, 2025), a slide states that Merrill | Private Bank is "Serving the wealth continuum of clients." [BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#9]
- C5 Merrill: In Global Wealth & Investment Management investor-day materials (published November 5, 2025), Bank of America's Global Wealth & Investment Management foundational beliefs state that "the Wealth Continuum allows us to deliver as one company and is our engine for lasting growth." [BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#8]

- A7 The Charles Schwab Corporation: In its 2025 Form 10-K, Schwab said it offers "Schwab Private Client Services (for clients with $1 million to $10 million in qualifying assets) and Schwab Private Wealth Services (for clients with more than $10 million)" as enhanced, relationship-based experiences. - A9 Raymond James Financial: Trade press reports that on September 29, 2026, Private Client Group President Tash Elwyn said Raymond James is committed to being a destination for private wealth advisors and the clients they serve, and that individuals and families with significant wealth require truly personal advice, backed by the right resources, to navigate their financial opportunities and complexities. ### Q13 (answer)

- C1 Morgan Stanley: On the 4Q24 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2024), management said Morgan Stanley has an exclusive partnership with Carta, under which Carta will refer private stock plan companies to Morgan Stanley as they move into going public, and the firm is committed to making it a seamless experience for those clients: "we have an exclusive partnership with Carta, which means that they will refer those private different stock plans or different corporate companies to Morgan Stanley as they move into going public." [MS-TR-2024Q4#77]
- C2 Morgan Stanley: On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), management said Morgan Stanley at Work, with its exclusive Carta partnership, positions the firm as an early trusted advisor to over 50,000 private companies, giving it early relationships as workplace companies grow: "Our Morgan Stanley at Work business, with its exclusive partnership with Carta, positions us as an early trusted advisor to over 50,000 private companies." [MS-TR-2025Q4#12]
- C3 Morgan Stanley: On the 3Q25 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025), management said that as IPOs come to market, workplace participants bring their assets to Morgan Stanley, dropping them into self-directed accounts and also moving them directly into advisor-led accounts: "People are bringing their assets to Morgan Stanley. They're dropping into their self-directed accounts, yes, but they're also moving it directly into the advisor-led accounts." [MS-TR-2025Q3#24]
- C4 Morgan Stanley: On the 4Q24 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2024), management defined channel migration as workplace assets that began in the workplace channel moving into the advisor-led channel: "what I mean by channel migration is we have Workplace assets that began at some point in Workplace, and we're seeing them move into the advisor-led concept." [MS-TR-2024Q4#94]
- C5 Morgan Stanley: On the 3Q25 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025), management said Morgan Stanley uses LeadIQ, an AI-powered lead distribution platform, to match workplace and self-directed relationships and facilitate engagement with financial advisors: "LeadIQ, our AI-powered lead distribution platform, focusing on matching workplace and self-directed relationships and facilitating engagement with our financial advisors." [MS-TR-2025Q3#8]
- C6 Morgan Stanley: On the 2Q26 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2026), management said the firm invests in referral models and products like LeadIQ that match individuals with advisers, with the goal of becoming their principal financial adviser: "They're going into creating our referral models. They're going into products like LeadIQ that will allow us to match individuals with advisers. And our goal is to be their principal financial adviser." [MS-TR-2026Q2#23]
- C7 Morgan Stanley: On the 4Q24 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2024), management said the Integrated Firm reaches employees through financial wellness programs for client companies' employees: "spanning from the advice dispensed in corporate boardrooms to our financial wellness programs for that company's employees." [MS-TR-2024Q4#13]
- C8 Morgan Stanley: On the 3Q25 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025), management described workplace as "probably the most exciting part" and said the firm is "just scratching the surface" of what it has seen in workplace. [MS-TR-2025Q3#24]
- C9 Morgan Stanley: On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), management said financial advisors, Workplace, and E*TRADE together comprise the firm's strategic client acquisition model, each a category leader: "Our financial advisors, Workplace, and E*TRADE, each a category leader, which taken together comprise our strategic client acquisition model." [MS-TR-2025Q4#7]
- C10 Morgan Stanley: On the 2Q26 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2026), management said growth led by workplace relationships is a cornerstone of the firm's client acquisition funnel: "Growth led by workplace relationships reflects a cornerstone of our client acquisition funnel." [MS-TR-2026Q2#3]
- C11 Morgan Stanley: On the 2Q25 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2025), management described the expansion of the essential workplace channel as an engine for future growth: "the expansion of our essential workplace channel, serve as an engine for future growth." [MS-TR-2025Q2#3]
- C12 Morgan Stanley: On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), management said the stock administration side of workplace has appeal both for the CEO and for the firm's Wealth Management business, and said the firm is quite excited about it: "Stock administration side of workplace has appeal both for the CEO and then for that firm for their Wealth Management business. So we're quite excited about it." [MS-TR-2025Q4#52]
- C13 Morgan Stanley: On the 3Q26 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), management described a differentiated workplace offering that continues to expand and source new relationships: "a differentiated workplace offering that continues to expand and source new relationships." [MS-TR-2026Q3#32]
- C14 Morgan Stanley: On the 2Q25 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2025), management described the Solium acquisition as "the linchpin to workplace" and E*TRADE as "filling out the funnel": "we can make an acquisition of Solium before people understand that that is the linchpin to workplace, if we can add E*TRADE as filling out the funnel" [MS-TR-2025Q2#48]
- C15 Morgan Stanley: On the 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), management said advisor-led assets originating from workplace and E*TRADE relationships grew to a record $99 billion for the full year 2025, compared with historical averages of around $60 billion per year: "Advisor-led assets originating from workplace and E*TRADE relationships accelerated, growing to a record $99 billion for the full year, compared to historical averages of around $60 billion per year." [MS-TR-2025Q4#20]

### Q14 (answer)

- C1 Raymond James's recruiting page (page content as of April 21, 2026) lists the AdvisorChoice® affiliation options as Traditional Employee, Advisor Select, Independent Contractor, Bank & Credit Union, and Corporate RIA, describing Traditional Employee as offering book ownership, Advisor Select as leading one's own branch, Independent Contractor as operating as an independent financial advisor, Bank & Credit Union as leveraging national strength, and Corporate RIA as exercising true autonomy. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#0]
- C2 The same recruiting page (content as of April 21, 2026) states the affiliation spectrum also includes an RIA & Custody Services option, described as 'Take strategic, financial and administrative control of your practice with a full-service partner at the ready.' [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C3 A graphic in the investor-day deck (published June 5, 2025) lists the AdvisorChoice spectrum as Employee Advisor (RJA), Advisor Select, Independent Advisor (ICD), Banks and Credit Unions, IAR (Corporate RIA), and RIA & Custody Services. [RJF-DECK-2025-investor-day#38]
- C4 The 2025 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states that financial advisors primarily affiliate directly as either employees or independent contractors, or as employees of third-party RIAs and broker-dealers served through the RIA and Custody Services (RCS) division. [RJF-10K-2025#10]
- C5 For the Traditional Employee option, the recruiting page (content as of April 21, 2026) states a payout of up to 50% plus a full array of competitive employee benefits, with compliance support provided by the branch manager and the home office. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C6 For the Advisor Select option, the same page (content as of April 21, 2026) states a 70-80% gross payout and a full array of competitive employee benefits. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C7 For the Independent Contractor option, the same page (content as of April 21, 2026) states an 81% to 90% payout, with benefits created through third-party partnerships. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C8 For the Bank & Credit Union option, the recruiting page (content as of April 21, 2026) states advisors receive a competitive compensation package from the financial institution in partnership with Raymond James. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#2]
- C9 For the Corporate RIA option, the recruiting page (content as of April 21, 2026) states an 81% to 90% payout, with benefits created through third-party partnerships. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#2]
- C10 For the RIA & Custody Services option, the recruiting page (content as of April 21, 2026) states advisors work with their RIA or broker/dealer to determine compensation and create their own benefits package. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#2]
- C11 The 2025 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states that compensation for employee financial advisors primarily includes a payout on the revenues they generate, and that such advisors also participate in the firm's employee benefit plans. [RJF-10K-2025#10]
- C12 On the earnings call for the quarter ended March 31, 2026 (published April 22, 2026), management explained that independent payouts are higher because independent advisors cover their own overhead costs, such as real estate and health insurance, and that even the employee side has tiered payout systems where advisors move higher on the payout grid as production increases. [RJF-TR-2026Q1#44]
- C13 The 2025 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states that Raymond James participates, with limited exceptions, in the Protocol for Broker Recruiting, a voluntary agreement among many firms that governs the client information financial advisors may take with them when they affiliate with a new firm and their ability to solicit clients of their prior firm. [RJF-10K-2025#91]
- C14 The same 10-K describes an industry-wide trend in which financial advisors are departing traditional firms, including to form independent RIAs or to join existing third-party RIAs, some of which are backed by private equity investors. [RJF-10K-2025#90]
- C15 The recruiting page (content as of April 21, 2026) describes AdvisorChoice® as a platform of dynamic options and the freedom to choose among them, letting advisors match their professional goals to the affiliation option that is right for them, with each option offering a unique combination of support, freedom, and flexibility. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#0]
- C16 On the earnings call for the quarter ended June 30, 2026 (published July 22, 2026), management said Raymond James has the largest addressable market in the industry because it has all the affiliation options an advisor could want and is agnostic—indifferent—about how advisors affiliate with the firm. [RJF-TR-2026Q2#26]
- C17 On the earnings call for the quarter ended June 30, 2025 (published July 23, 2025), management described the value proposition as 'best of both worlds'—an advisor- and client-focused culture coupled with leading technology and solutions—and said it continues to resonate with advisors across all affiliation options. [RJF-TR-2025Q2#7]
- C18 On the earnings call for the quarter ended December 31, 2025 (published January 28, 2026), management said the firm has the largest addressable market across its affiliation options, from employee to independent contractor to RIA custody, with critical mass and decades of experience in all of them. [RJF-TR-2025Q4#50]
- C19 On the earnings call for the quarter ended March 31, 2026 (published April 22, 2026), management stated its focus is to remain the absolute best destination for financial advisers and their clients across all of its affiliation options. [RJF-TR-2026Q1#23]
- C20 On the earnings call for the quarter ended June 30, 2026 (published July 22, 2026), management said growth remains focused on quality over quantity. [RJF-TR-2026Q2#5]
- C21 On the earnings call for the quarter ended June 30, 2026 (published July 22, 2026), management said recruiting is not something that can be turned on and off quarter to quarter, and requires consistency in retaining existing advisers, recruiting new advisers, and having the team focused on finding advisers who are good cultural fits with the firm across its affiliation options. [RJF-TR-2026Q2#60]
- C22 On the earnings call for the quarter ended December 31, 2025 (published January 28, 2026), management said recruited advisors come from competitor types including wires, regionals, and other independents, and described those firms as friendly competitors who are doing good jobs keeping their advisors. [RJF-TR-2025Q4#50]
- C23 On the earnings call for the quarter ended June 30, 2025 (published July 23, 2025), management identified private equity-backed roll-ups as a relatively novel competitive dynamic in advisor recruiting, saying they have been extremely aggressive in trading and paying at higher and higher multiples over the last three years in particular. [RJF-TR-2025Q2#44]
- C24 The 2025 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) names its direct competitors as other national full service broker-dealers, investment banking firms, commercial banks, investment advisors, investment managers, and, to a lesser extent, discount brokers and dealers, and notes competition from more recent entrants including fintechs. [RJF-10K-2025#109]
- C25 On the earnings call for the quarter ended September 30, 2024 (published October 23, 2024), management said a couple of OSJ relationships in the independent contractor division had decided to leave the platform, and roughly $3 billion of AUA left the firm in the fiscal fourth quarter. [RJF-TR-2024Q3#10]
- C26 On the same call, the firm anticipated approximately $5 billion of assets associated with those departing OSJ firms would complete their transfers off the platform in early fiscal 2025. [RJF-TR-2024Q3#10]
- C27 On the earnings call for the quarter ended September 30, 2025 (published October 22, 2025), management said the firm would lose a platform through bank M&A the next quarter, approximately $2.7 billion of assets. [RJF-TR-2025Q3#46]
- C28 On the earnings call for the quarter ended December 31, 2024 (published January 29, 2025), management said the large departure previously discussed had essentially almost totally gone from the platform, and that it does not see anything big on the leaving side, though there is always some attrition. [RJF-TR-2024Q4#49]
- C29 The 2025 Form 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) warns that financial advisors may leave the firm, including for independent RIAs or third-party RIAs, and that retiring financial advisors without a successor affiliated with the firm may sell their practices to unaffiliated third parties, which could negatively affect AUA. [RJF-10K-2025#90]
- A1 As of September 30, 2025, Raymond James had 8,943 employee and independent contractor financial advisors affiliated with it in the Private Client Group, per the 2025 Form 10-K. [RJF-10K-2025#9]
- A2 Total client assets under administration (AUA) in the Private Client Group segment were $1.67 trillion as of September 30, 2025, per the 2025 Form 10-K. [RJF-10K-2025#9]
- A3 Fee-based client assets under administration in the Private Client Group were $1.01 trillion as of September 30, 2025, per the 2025 Form 10-K. [RJF-10K-2025#9]
