- A5 As of June 30, 2026, assets associated with firms affiliated through the RCS division totaled $251.3 billion (compared with $228.2 billion at March 31, 2026 and $201.6 billion at June 30, 2025), per the Form 10-Q for the period ended June 30, 2026. [RJF-10Q-2026Q2#307]
- A6 The Financial Institutions Division has 940 advisors across 160 financial institutions, per the company's bank and credit union advisors page (content as of May 17, 2026). [RJF-PR-web-bank-and-credit-union-advisors#0]
- A7 Financial Institutions Division advisors average $166 million in assets under management per advisor, per the same page (content as of May 17, 2026). [RJF-PR-web-bank-and-credit-union-advisors#0]
- A8 Financial Institutions Division advisors average $950,000 in advisor production, per the same page (content as of May 17, 2026). [RJF-PR-web-bank-and-credit-union-advisors#0]
- A9 In fiscal 2025, recruited financial advisors to the domestic independent contractor and employee channels had trailing 12-month production at their previous firms totaling $407 million, which the firm described as record recruiting results, per the earnings call for the quarter ended September 30, 2025. [RJF-TR-2025Q3#7]
- A10 In fiscal 2025, those recruited financial advisors to the domestic independent contractor and employee channels had approximately $58 billion of client assets at their previous firms, per the earnings call for the quarter ended September 30, 2025. [RJF-TR-2025Q3#7]
- A11 Including assets recruited into the RIA and Custody Services division, the firm recruited total client assets of nearly $63 billion across all platforms over the 12 months of fiscal 2025, per the earnings call for the quarter ended September 30, 2025. [RJF-TR-2025Q3#7]
- A12 In the fiscal second quarter of 2026, recruited financial advisors to the domestic independent contractor and employee channels had trailing 12-month production totaling $141 million, per the earnings call for the quarter ended March 31, 2026. [RJF-TR-2026Q1#4]
- A13 In the fiscal second quarter of 2026, those recruited financial advisors had nearly $21 billion of client assets at their previous firms, per the earnings call for the quarter ended March 31, 2026. [RJF-TR-2026Q1#4]
- A14 In the fiscal third quarter of 2026, recruited financial advisors to the domestic independent contractor and employee channels had trailing 12-month production totaling $156 million, per the earnings call for the quarter ended June 30, 2026. [RJF-TR-2026Q2#5]
- A15 In the fiscal third quarter of 2026, those recruited financial advisors had nearly $23 billion of client assets at their previous firms, per the earnings call for the quarter ended June 30, 2026. [RJF-TR-2026Q2#5]
- A16 Through the first nine months of fiscal 2026, recruited advisers had trailing 12-month production totaling $393 million, per the earnings call for the quarter ended June 30, 2026. [RJF-TR-2026Q2#6]
- A17 Through the first nine months of fiscal 2026, those recruited advisers had more than $56 billion of client assets at their previous firms, per the earnings call for the quarter ended June 30, 2026. [RJF-TR-2026Q2#6]

### Q15 (answer)

- C1 The Charles Schwab Corporation: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026) states that Schwab's largest sources of net revenues are net interest revenue, asset management and administration fees, trading revenue, and bank deposit account fees, and that these revenue streams are supported by the combination of its broker-dealer, bank, and asset management operating subsidiaries. [SCHW-10K-2025#27]
- C2 The Charles Schwab Corporation: The 2025 10-K states that net interest revenue is the difference between interest generated on interest-earning assets and interest paid on funding sources, that Schwab's primary funding source for interest-earning assets is uninvested client cash balances held on its balance sheet, and that its interest-earning assets are primarily high-quality fixed income securities, margin loans, and bank loans. [SCHW-10K-2025#27]
- C3 The Charles Schwab Corporation: The 2025 10-K states that asset management and administration fees are primarily earned from proprietary money market mutual funds, proprietary and third-party mutual funds and ETFs, and fee-based managed investing solutions. [SCHW-10K-2025#27]
- C4 The Charles Schwab Corporation: The 2025 10-K states that trading revenue includes commissions earned for executing trades for clients in certain individual equities, options, futures, fixed income securities, and certain third-party mutual funds and ETFs, order flow revenue, and principal transactions revenue earned primarily from actions to support client trading in fixed income securities. [SCHW-10K-2025#27]
- C5 The Charles Schwab Corporation: Trade press reporting published September 30, 2026 states that in Q2 2026 Schwab generated nearly $3.4 billion in net interest revenue, largely on approximately $485.7 billion held in sweep accounts, according to Fitch. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-09-30-IN-01#1]
- C6 The Charles Schwab Corporation: The Q2 2026 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2026, published August 7, 2026) states that bank deposit account fees increased in the second quarter of 2026 primarily due to improved net yields, partially offset by lower average bank deposit account balances. [SCHW-10Q-2026Q2#84]
- C7 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 19, 2026) states that, within Wealth Management, fee-based client assets represent the amount of client assets where the basis of payment for services is a fee calculated on those assets. [MS-10K-2025#167]
- C8 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 10-K states that, within Wealth Management, advisor-led client assets represent client assets in accounts that have a Wealth Management representative assigned. [MS-10K-2025#167]
- C9 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 10-K states that within Wealth Management, asset management revenues are related to advisory services associated with fee-based assets, account service and administration, and distribution of products, and are generally based on the net asset value of the account in which a client is invested. [MS-10K-2025#147]
- C10 Morgan Stanley: The Q2 2025 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2025, published August 4, 2025) reports an average fee rate of 62 basis points on total fee-based client assets for the three months ended June 30, 2025, based on asset management revenues related to advisory services associated with fee-based assets. [MS-10Q-2025Q2#69]
- C11 Morgan Stanley: The Q2 2025 10-Q reports an average fee rate of 63 basis points on total fee-based client assets for the three months ended June 30, 2024, based on asset management revenues related to advisory services associated with fee-based assets. [MS-10Q-2025Q2#69]
- C12 Morgan Stanley: The Q1 2026 10-Q (period ended March 31, 2026, published May 5, 2026) reports an average fee rate of 62 basis points on total fee-based client assets for the three months ended March 31, 2026, based on asset management revenues related to advisory services associated with fee-based assets. [MS-10Q-2026Q1#59]
- C13 Morgan Stanley: The Q1 2026 10-Q reports an average fee rate of 63 basis points on total fee-based client assets for the three months ended March 31, 2025, based on asset management revenues related to advisory services associated with fee-based assets. [MS-10Q-2026Q1#59]
- C14 Morgan Stanley: The Q1 2026 10-Q reports that fee-based client assets were 48% of advisor-led client assets at March 31, 2026, within the Wealth Management advisor-led channel. [MS-10Q-2026Q1#51]
- C15 Morgan Stanley: The Q3 2025 10-Q (period ended September 30, 2025, published November 3, 2025) reports that fee-based client assets were 49% of advisor-led client assets at September 30, 2025, within the Wealth Management advisor-led channel. [MS-10Q-2025Q3#58]
- C16 Edward Jones: The Q2 2026 10-Q (period ended June 26, 2026, published August 7, 2026) states that Edward Jones primarily derives revenues from fees for providing investment advisory and other account services to clients, fees for assets held by clients, and commissions for distributing mutual fund shares and insurance products and for securities transactions. [EDJ-10Q-2026Q2#28]
- C17 Edward Jones: The Q3 2025 10-Q (period ended September 26, 2025, published November 7, 2025) states that, within fee revenue, Edward Jones's asset-based fees include advisory program fees based on the average daily market value of client assets in the program and contractual rates, and that these fees are affected by changes in market values and by client dollars invested in and divested from the accounts. [EDJ-10Q-2025Q3#62]
- C18 Edward Jones: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published March 13, 2026) states that Edward Jones earns revenue on clients' assets through service fees and revenue sharing received under agreements with certain mutual fund and insurance companies. [EDJ-10K-2025#23]
- C19 Edward Jones: The 2025 10-K states that, for mutual funds, Edward Jones's service fees are generally based on the average daily market value of client assets held in a company's mutual fund or insurance product, and that service fee revenue is recognized monthly once the transaction price is known and the services have been performed. [EDJ-10K-2025#208]
- C20 Edward Jones: The 2025 10-K states that Edward Jones's other asset-based fee revenue consists of revenue sharing, fund adviser fees, cash solutions and Trust Co. fees. [EDJ-10K-2025#208]
- C21 Edward Jones: The 2025 10-K states that Edward Jones earns cash solutions revenue from the Edward Jones Insured Bank Deposit Program, an interest-bearing cash management solution for clients. [EDJ-10K-2025#23]
- C22 Edward Jones: The 2025 10-K states that Edward Jones also earns certain asset-based fees and per-account fees from the Edward Jones Money Market Fund, some or all of which may be voluntarily waived, while its subsidiary Olive Street earns investment management fees as the fund's investment adviser. [EDJ-10K-2025#23]
- C23 Edward Jones: The 2025 10-K states that, in addition to advisory programs, Edward Jones earns asset-based fees from trust services and investment management services offered through Edward Jones Trust Company, a wholly-owned subsidiary. [EDJ-10K-2025#23]
- C24 Merrill: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 25, 2026) states that in 2025 Merrill Wealth Management revenue of $20.7 billion increased nine percent, primarily driven by higher asset management fees and higher brokerage fees from increased transactional volume. [BAC-10K-2025#247]
- C25 Raymond James Financial: The 2025 10-K (period ended September 30, 2025, published November 25, 2025) states that through its Private Client Group it provides financial planning, investment advisory, and securities transaction services for which it generally charges either asset-based fees, presented in 'Asset management and related administrative fees,' or sales commissions, presented in 'Brokerage revenues.' [RJF-10K-2025#216]
- C26 Raymond James Financial: The 2025 10-K states that its Private Client Group also earns revenues for distribution and related services performed related to mutual and other funds, fixed and variable annuities, and insurance products. [RJF-10K-2025#696]
- C27 Raymond James Financial: The 2025 10-K states that its Private Client Group includes servicing fee revenues from third-party mutual fund and annuity companies whose products it distributes and from banks to which it sweeps a portion of clients' cash deposits as part of the RJBDP multi-bank sweep program. [RJF-10K-2025#696]
- C28 Raymond James Financial: The 2025 10-K states that its Private Client Group also includes net interest earnings primarily on assets segregated for regulatory purposes, margin loans provided to clients, cash balances, and securities borrowing transactions, net of interest paid on client cash balances. [RJF-10K-2025#696]
- C29 Raymond James Financial: The Q4 2025 10-Q (period ended December 31, 2025, published February 6, 2026) states that the majority of assets in Private Client Group fee-based accounts are invested in programs for which its financial advisors provide investment advisory services, and that administrative services are generally performed by its Asset Management segment, which shares a portion of the related revenue. [RJF-10Q-2025Q4#237]
- C30 Raymond James Financial: The 2025 10-K states that its Asset Management segment earns asset management and related administrative fees for providing asset management, portfolio management and related administrative services to retail and institutional clients, and oversees the portion of fee-based AUA invested in managed programs for PCG clients through AMS. [RJF-10K-2025#240]
- C31 Morgan Stanley: On the Q4 2025 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025, published January 15, 2026), management said that the drivers of continued expanded Wealth Management margins are twofold: building fee-based revenues and fee-based assets by introducing clients to the advice-based (advisor-led) model, while self-directed clients generate increasing transactional revenues through investments in E*TRADE. [MS-TR-2025Q4#34]
- C32 The Charles Schwab Corporation: Trade press reporting published September 30, 2026 states that wealth managers earn revenue on client cash by investing or lending uninvested balances at yields higher than the rates they pay clients, and that when interest rates climb, yields on loans, floating-rate assets, and reinvested fixed-income securities rise, widening the spread that drives net interest income. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-09-30-IN-01#0]
- C33 The Charles Schwab Corporation: Trade press reporting published September 30, 2026 states that Fitch said firms with greater exposure to floating-rate assets, faster reinvestment of maturing holdings, and less reliance on rate-sensitive funding are better positioned to see more immediate net interest income gains. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-09-30-IN-01#0]
- C34 The Charles Schwab Corporation: Trade press reporting published September 30, 2026 states that Raymond James, which holds primarily floating-rate securities and corporate loans alongside certain variable-rate sweep fee arrangements with third-party banks, is positioned to see more immediate earnings benefits when rates rise because its repricing happens faster in response to short-term rate movements. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-09-30-IN-01#2]
- C35 The Charles Schwab Corporation: Trade press reporting published September 30, 2026 states that LPL, which does not operate its own bank and instead places client deposits with partner institutions that pay it a fee, holds roughly 40% of insured cash account balances under variable-rate agreements as of the second quarter of 2026, so a portion of its earnings reprices relatively quickly while the fixed-rate segment lags. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-09-30-IN-01#2]
- C36 The Charles Schwab Corporation: Trade press reporting published September 30, 2026 states that Ameriprise's bank investment portfolio, with a 4.2-year duration and 6% in floating-rate securities, is weighted toward fixed-rate assets, meaning repricing would be more gradual. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-09-30-IN-01#2]
- C37 The Charles Schwab Corporation: Trade press reporting published September 30, 2026 states that Stifel's commercial, fund-banking and venture deposit mix makes it harder to isolate the revenue contribution from sweep balances specifically, according to Fitch. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-09-30-IN-01#2]
- C38 The Charles Schwab Corporation: On the Q4 2025 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025, published January 21, 2026), the CEO said that return on client assets (ROCA) for Managed Investing solutions is two times that of retail, which diversifies the firm's revenue streams. [SCHW-TR-2025Q4#14]
- C39 The Charles Schwab Corporation: The Q3 2025 10-Q (period ended September 30, 2025, published November 7, 2025) states that Schwab's Investor Services segment provides retail brokerage, investment advisory, and banking and trust services to individual investors, and retirement plan and business services, as well as other corporate brokerage services, to businesses and their employees. [SCHW-10Q-2025Q3#311]
- C40 The Charles Schwab Corporation: The Q3 2025 10-Q states that Schwab's Advisor Services segment provides custodial, trading, banking and trust, and support services to independent RIAs, independent retirement advisors, and recordkeepers, and that revenues and expenses are attributed to the two segments based on which segment services the client. [SCHW-10Q-2025Q3#311]
- C41 Merrill: The 2025 10-K states that client balances managed under advisory and/or discretion of GWIM are AUM and are typically held in diversified portfolios, that fees earned on AUM are calculated as a percentage of clients' AUM balances, and that asset management fees commonly depend on the breadth of the client's relationship. [BAC-10K-2025#249]
- C42 Merrill: On the Q2 2026 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2026, published July 14, 2026), the CFO stated that the growth in banking relationships and lending balances demonstrates the power of the firm's integrated wealth and banking model, as clients consolidate more of their financial lives with Bank of America. [BAC-TR-2026Q2#20]
- C43 Merrill: The November 5, 2025 investor day deck states that Merrill's foundational beliefs include that growth of fully advised client relationships drives financial outcomes and that the resilience of the business model creates enduring shareholder value. [BAC-DECK-2025-investor-day#8]
- C44 Raymond James Financial: The 2025 10-K states that Private Client Group asset management and related administrative fees and brokerage revenues are typically correlated with the level of PCG client assets under administration, including those in fee-based accounts, as well as the overall U.S. equity markets. [RJF-10K-2025#216]
- C45 Raymond James Financial: The Q2 2026 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2026, published August 5, 2026) states that for the quarter ended June 30, 2026, Private Client Group asset management and related administrative fees increased primarily due to higher assets in fee-based accounts resulting from market appreciation and net new assets driven by financial advisor recruiting and retention, while brokerage revenues increased primarily due to higher client activity and higher trailing revenues from higher client asset values. [RJF-10Q-2026Q2#315]
- C46 Raymond James Financial: On the Q2 2025 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2025, published July 23, 2025), management said that about 65% of its overall Private Client Group assets are fee-based, and that fee-based assets have grown faster than overall client assets. [RJF-TR-2025Q2#62]
- C47 Raymond James Financial: The Q2 2025 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2025, published August 6, 2025) states that Private Client Group results can be impacted by changes in the level of client cash balances and by the allocation of client cash balances between the RJBDP, the CIP, and the ESP, because the PCG segment may earn different amounts from each of these client cash destinations. [RJF-10Q-2025Q2#302]
- C48 Raymond James Financial: The Q1 2026 10-Q (period ended March 31, 2026, published May 6, 2026) states that for the quarter ended March 31, 2026, Private Client Group account and service fees decreased due to a decrease in RJBDP fees paid to PCG from third-party banks reflecting lower average balances swept and a lower average RJBDP third-party bank yield, partially offset by higher mutual fund service fees from higher average mutual fund assets. [RJF-10Q-2026Q1#306]
- C49 Raymond James Financial: The Q2 2026 10-Q states that PCG servicing fees for clients' deposits swept to banks as part of the RJBDP are variable and fluctuate based on client cash balances in the program, the level of short-term interest rates, and the interest paid to clients. [RJF-10Q-2026Q2#313]
- C50 The comparison can be made at a qualitative level on this evidence, but not as a like-for-like quantitative comparison across firms. Schwab, the direct-to-investor brokerage and bank, earns through net interest revenue on client cash, asset management and administration fees, trading revenue, and bank deposit account fees. The advisor-led firms earn primarily through fee-based advice revenue tied to client assets: Morgan Stanley's Wealth Management asset management revenues are generally based on the net asset value of the account in which a client is invested; Edward Jones primarily derives revenues from investment advisory and other account service fees, asset-based fees, and commissions; Merrill's fees on AUM are calculated as a percentage of clients' AUM balances; and Raymond James generally charges either asset-based fees or sales commissions in its Private Client Group. The evidence states the driver directly: Morgan Stanley attributes continued expanded Wealth Management margins to building fee-based revenues and fee-based assets in the advisor-led model while self-directed clients generate transactional revenue, and trade reporting notes that wealth managers earn on client cash by investing or lending uninvested balances at yields above what they pay clients. Edward Jones has no driver-specific statement in the provided sources, so the comparison is incomplete for that firm. A like-for-like quantitative comparison is not valid because the firms use different definitions, scopes, units, and periods; for example, Morgan Stanley's basis-point fee rates on Wealth Management fee-based client assets, Raymond James's Private Client Group figures, Merrill's GWIM figures, and Schwab's consolidated revenue sources cannot be ranked or compared on a common basis. [SCHW-10K-2025#27, MS-10K-2025#147, MS-10Q-2025Q2#69, EDJ-10Q-2026Q2#28, BAC-10K-2025#249, BAC-10K-2025#244, RJF-10K-2025#216, MS-TR-2025Q4#34, SCHW-TRADE-2026-09-30-IN-01#0]
- A1 Merrill: On the Q2 2025 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2025, published July 16, 2025), the CFO stated that the wealth management business is primarily a fee-based advice-driven model, with about 30 percent of revenue coming from net interest income derived from GWIM clients and their loan and deposit balances. [BAC-TR-2025Q2#35]
- A2 Merrill: On the Q4 2024 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2024, published January 16, 2025), the CFO stated that about 30 percent of GWIM revenue remained in net interest income, which complements the fees earned in the advice model. [BAC-TR-2024Q4#30]
- A3 Merrill: The 2025 10-K states that in 2025 GWIM asset management fees increased 12 percent to $15.4 billion, reflecting higher market valuations and positive AUM flows, while higher brokerage fees reflected increased transactional volume. [BAC-10K-2025#246]
- A4 Merrill: The 2025 10-K states that in 2025 GWIM noninterest income, which primarily includes investment and brokerage services income, increased $1.7 billion to $17.7 billion. [BAC-10K-2025#246]
- A5 Merrill: The 2025 10-K states that in 2025 GWIM net interest income was $7,197 million. [BAC-10K-2025#244]
- A6 Merrill: The 2025 10-K states that in 2025 GWIM investment and brokerage services income was $17,019 million. [BAC-10K-2025#244]
- G Edward Jones: no evidence found in the provided sources on what drives the difference between how its advisor-led model earns on client assets and how Schwab's direct-to-investor model earns.

### Q20 (answer)

- C1 UBS: UBS's research report (published April 22, 2026) reports that when choosing a wealth manager, experience and expertise sit at the top of the next generation's wish list, at 79%. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#83]
- C2 UBS: The same UBS research report (published April 22, 2026) reports that 41% of next-generation respondents value a wealth manager's ability to fully understand the next generation's needs. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#83]
- C3 UBS: The same UBS research report (published April 22, 2026) reports priorities vary by gender among next-generation respondents: women emphasize wealth managers' experience (90%), frequent engagement (35%), and early commitment to the next generation (40%), while men are more likely to prioritize advisors with a deep understanding of their financial goals (49%). [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#83]
- C4 Cerulli: Cerulli's research (published October 29, 2025) reports that younger investors typically are more comfortable directing their own investing through online brokerages, so financial advisors looking to connect with these prospective clients must be able to articulate benefits outside of the investing realm, such as financial planning, lending, and tax services. [CERULLI-GWT-2025#23]
- C5 Cerulli: Cerulli's research (published October 29, 2025) reports that while older investors tend to be more advisor-reliant, younger investors prefer to retain a greater degree of control of at least part of their investing lives. [CERULLI-GWT-2025#1]
- C6 Fidelity: Fidelity's 2025 Investor Insights Study (published October 9, 2025) finds wealthy next-generation households are reshaping the advisor-client relationship with a focus on holistic financial planning, life goal achievement, and non-traditional investment strategies. [FIDELITY-III-2025#0]
- C7 UBS: UBS's research report (published April 22, 2026) finds the next generation rarely relies on a single source of guidance for succession planning; peer insights are most popular (27%), just ahead of wealth managers (21%), with tax advisors (16%), lawyers (14%), and family officers (13%) ranking roughly even. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#78]
- C8 UBS: UBS's research report (published April 22, 2026) finds younger adults (22 to 25 years) rely most on peers (57%) for succession-planning advice, while older respondents turn increasingly to formal legal advice. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#81]
- C9 FINRA: FINRA investor research (published December 4, 2025) finds investors under 35 are somewhat less likely than older investors to rely on financial professionals and much more likely to use information from friends, family and colleagues; word of mouth from friends/family/colleagues is the most frequently mentioned source of investment information, used by 85% of investors under 35. [FINRAF-INVESTORS-2025#45]
- C10 Schwab: On Schwab's Q3 2025 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025; transcript published October 16, 2025), the CEO stated that Gen Z learn about finances primarily through social media and family, and that Schwab meets them in the channels where they turn for information about investing. [SCHW-TR-2025Q3#11]
- C11 Wealthtender: Wealthtender's survey (published August 13, 2025; 2025 survey of respondents ages 35-64) finds the most-planned resource for finding a financial advisor is referrals from friends or family (62%), with search engines such as Google or Bing next (49.8%). [WEALTHTENDER-ADVISOR-SEARCH-2025#8]
- C12 Wealthtender: Wealthtender's survey (published August 13, 2025; 2025 survey of respondents ages 35-64) finds 25% plan to use AI tools like ChatGPT to start their advisor search; about one-third (32%) will use online advisor directories; social media platforms influence about 22%; and educational events (online webinars 19%, in-person seminars 18%) also play a role. [WEALTHTENDER-ADVISOR-SEARCH-2025#5]
- C13 Bank of America Private Bank: Bank of America Private Bank's 2026 Study of Wealthy Americans (published June 17, 2026) finds 65% of younger investors still prefer to receive investment advice from a human advisor; the study defines younger investors as Gen Z and Millennials, ages 21 to 45. [BOFA-PB-STUDY-2026#5, BOFA-PB-STUDY-2026#14]
- C14 Bank of America Private Bank: The same 2026 study finds 47% of young investors use AI to research companies or markets and 87% are comfortable with advisors using AI to help manage portfolios. [BOFA-PB-STUDY-2026#5, BOFA-PB-STUDY-2026#14]
- C15 UBS: UBS's research report (published April 22, 2026) finds most next-generation respondents favor a blended approach to communicating (83%), combining digital convenience with in-person interaction. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#84]
- C16 UBS: UBS's research report (published April 22, 2026) finds two-thirds (67%) of next-generation respondents say a strong personal connection with their advisor is important. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#84]
- C17 UBS: UBS's research report (published April 22, 2026) finds more than half (56%) of next-generation respondents want advisors who can build trusted relationships across generations. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#83]
- C18 UBS: UBS's research report (published April 22, 2026) finds the next generation want advisors with not just technical know-how, but the ability to build rapport with the family and communicate clearly with every generation. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#76]
- C19 UBS: UBS's research report (published April 22, 2026) finds continuity is important in succession planning: 41% of next-generation respondents prefer to work with their family's existing bank, while 31% want a new provider and 29% are open to switching. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#81]
- C20 UBS: UBS's research report (published April 22, 2026) quotes a next-generation portfolio manager saying good partners aren't easy to find, and when they find someone committed and trustworthy they want to work with them for the long term. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#81]
- C21 Wealthtender: Wealthtender's survey (published August 13, 2025; 2025 survey of respondents ages 35-64) finds nearly half of participants (48%) said pricing and overall anticipated costs are among the top three factors leading them to choose one advisor over another. [WEALTHTENDER-ADVISOR-SEARCH-2025#37]
- C22 Wealthtender: Wealthtender's survey (published August 13, 2025; 2025 survey of respondents ages 35-64) finds that before contacting an advisor, 62% of people want to know the advisor's fee structure first, alongside areas of specialization (64%). [WEALTHTENDER-ADVISOR-SEARCH-2025#30]
- C23 Wealthtender: Wealthtender's survey (published August 13, 2025; 2025 survey of respondents ages 35-64) finds fee transparency tops the trust list: 73% of participants said transparency in fees and services is crucial for establishing an advisor's credibility. [WEALTHTENDER-ADVISOR-SEARCH-2025#15]
- C24 Bank of America Private Bank: Bank of America Private Bank's 2026 Study of Wealthy Americans (published June 17, 2026) was an online survey by Escalent among 1,431 wealthy U.S. individuals aged 21 or older with at least $3 million in investable assets (excluding primary residence), fielded January 8 to February 5, 2026. [BOFA-PB-STUDY-2026#17]
- C25 Bank of America Private Bank: The 2026 Bank of America Private Bank Study of Wealthy Americans finds 65% of younger investors still prefer to receive investment advice from a human advisor, 47% use AI to research companies or markets, and 87% are comfortable with advisors using AI. [BOFA-PB-STUDY-2026#5, BOFA-PB-STUDY-2026#14]
- C26 Bank of America Private Bank: The 2024 Bank of America Private Bank Study of Wealthy Americans (published June 18, 2024) was an online survey by Escalent among 1,007 HNW U.S. respondents aged at least 21 with at least $3 million in investable assets (excluding primary residence), with a margin of error of +/-3% at 95% confidence. [BOFA-PB-STUDY-2024#12]
- C27 Fidelity: Fidelity's 2025 Investor Insights Study (published October 9, 2025) compares the evolving preferences and expectations of wealthy next-generation households with those of baby boomers. [FIDELITY-III-2025#0]
- C28 Wealthtender: The Wealthtender 2025 study (published August 13, 2025) surveyed 500 U.S. adults with household incomes over $100,000, selected from 1,557 screened individuals ages 35-64, focused on those who don't have a financial advisor or are considering a change and anticipate hiring an advisor within five years. [WEALTHTENDER-ADVISOR-SEARCH-2025#60]
- C29 Wealthtender: Wealthtender's 2025 study key takeaways include: 97% plan to interview multiple advisors and 96% will do further research online before hiring; 83% research the advisor's reputation via online reviews and awards; the two most important pre-contact pieces of information are areas of specialization (64%) and fee/pricing structure (62%). [WEALTHTENDER-ADVISOR-SEARCH-2025#2]
- C30 UBS: UBS's research report on what the next generation looks for in a wealth manager (published April 22, 2026) finds experience and expertise top the next generation's wish list (79%), with 56% wanting advisors who build trusted relationships across generations. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#83]

### Q21 (answer)

- C1 Industry research: Cerulli (published October 29, 2025) projects households will transfer $123.7 trillion to heirs ($105 trillion) and charities ($18 trillion) between now and 2048. [CERULLI-GWT-2025#2]
- C2 Industry research: Cerulli (published October 29, 2025) projects Generation Xers will inherit $39 trillion of the transferred wealth. [CERULLI-GWT-2025#2]
- C3 Industry research: Cerulli (published October 29, 2025) projects Millennials will inherit $46 trillion of the transferred wealth. [CERULLI-GWT-2025#2]
- C4 Industry research: Cerulli (published October 29, 2025) projects women in the Baby Boomer and older generations will receive $40 trillion in horizontal transfers as they outlive their husbands. [CERULLI-GWT-2025#2]
- C5 Industry research: Cerulli (published October 29, 2025) projects women in younger generations will receive $47 trillion of the wealth transferred intergenerationally. [CERULLI-GWT-2025#2]
- C6 Industry research: Cerulli (published October 29, 2025) estimates most wealth owned by older generations will be donated or passed down to Gen X or Millennial heirs, an estimated total of $85 trillion. [CERULLI-GWT-2025#13]
- C7 Industry research: UBS (published April 22, 2026) estimates USD 83 trillion in private wealth will change hands globally over the next two to three decades. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#6]
- C8 Industry research: Cerulli (published October 29, 2025) projects the wealth transfer will occur between now and 2048. [CERULLI-GWT-2025#2]
- C9 Industry research: UBS (published April 22, 2026) states the Great Wealth Transfer will occur over the next two to three decades. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#6]
- C10 Industry research: UBS (published April 22, 2026) states an estimated USD 83 trillion in assets is expected to pass from one generation to the next over the next two decades. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#12]
- C11 Schwab: On Schwab's 3Q26 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), the firm stated that roughly 80% of retail assets are held by ultra-high net worth and high net worth households, and that it is well positioned to continue serving future generations when that wealth is passed on. [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#11]
- C12 Industry research: Cerulli (published October 29, 2025) says wealth is now more likely to be held by older households, which will transfer it to heirs and charities. [CERULLI-GWT-2025#2]
- C13 Industry research: Cerulli (published October 29, 2025) says the next generation (Generation Xers and Millennials) will inherit, while women in Baby Boomer and older generations will receive horizontal transfers and women in younger generations will receive intergenerational transfers. [CERULLI-GWT-2025#2]
- C14 Industry research: UBS (published April 22, 2026) says wealth is expected to pass from one generation to the next, and the inheriting next generation often begins its involvement earlier through family discussions, governance structures, or growing responsibilities. [UBS-NEXTGEN-2026#12]
- C15 Schwab: Schwab's 3Q26 investor deck (period ended September 30, 2026) reports that approximately 75% of assets are retained in a wealth transfer event. [SCHW-DECK-2026Q3#24]
- C16 Schwab: On Schwab's 3Q26 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), the firm said its retention rate around wealth transfers is extremely high and that it is winning assets with all generations. [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#11]
- C17 Edward Jones: Edward Jones's 2Q25 10-Q (period ended June 27, 2025) states that net new assets decreased 10% compared to the second quarter of 2024, continuing a downward trend attributed to higher asset outflows, which are impacted by an aging client base and generational wealth transfers outside the firm. [EDJ-10Q-2025Q2#67]
- C18 Schwab: Schwab's 3Q26 investor deck (period ended September 30, 2026) describes the firm as built to win across generations. [SCHW-DECK-2026Q3#24]
- C19 Schwab: On Schwab's 3Q26 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), the firm said it is well positioned to serve future generations when wealth is passed on and is winning assets with all generations. [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#11]
- C20 Edward Jones: Edward Jones's 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) describes generational wealth transfer as one of its strategic risks, most notably competition for clients and personnel in light of the evolving financial services industry and generational wealth transfer and changing client demographics, preferences and values. [EDJ-10K-2025#304]
- C21 Edward Jones: Edward Jones's 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) says its continued ability to compete and adapt its business model may be adversely impacted by the evolving financial services industry and generational wealth transfer, including changing client expectations and demographics. [EDJ-10K-2025#56]
- C22 Schwab: On Schwab's 1Q25 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2025), the firm said it is expanding its branch footprint and hiring hundreds of financial consultants and wealth consultants to deepen relationships with its higher net worth retail clients and their families. [SCHW-TR-2025Q1#12]
- C23 Schwab: On Schwab's 3Q26 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2026), the firm said it is investing in people and technology to build, deepen, and retain trusted relationships now and for the decades to come. [SCHW-TR-2026Q3#12]
- C24 Schwab: On Schwab's 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), the firm said it has improved its tax, trust, and estate capabilities and built specialty teams for higher net worth clients. [SCHW-TR-2025Q4#13]
- C25 Edward Jones: Edward Jones's 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states it is making substantial investments in initiatives that include targeting new clients through High Net Worth, Workplace, and a new digital platform focused on attracting and retaining Next Generation investors. [EDJ-10K-2025#88]
- C26 Edward Jones: Edward Jones's 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states it has resulted in and expects to continue making significant investments in financial advisors and other human capital, technology infrastructure, digital initiatives, knowledge- and data-powered tools, and strategic relationships to support long-term growth objectives. [EDJ-10K-2025#56]
- C27 Raymond James: Trade press (September 29, 2026) reports Raymond James positions estate and charitable planning, lending and cash management, risk management, and intergenerational wealth strategies as tools advisors can use to serve households with complex financial profiles. [RJF-TRADE-2026-09-29-IN-01#3]
- C28 Raymond James: Trade press (September 29, 2026) reports Raymond James is building out its private wealth platform, including launching Private Wealth Guided Portfolios, to capture a larger share of the high-net-worth advisory market. [RJF-TRADE-2026-09-29-IN-01#0]
- C29 Raymond James: Raymond James's RIA and Custody Services web page (page content as of July 12, 2026) offers succession planning and acquisition support as part of its RIA and custody services resources. [RJF-PR-web-independent-ria-custody-services#2]
- C30 Raymond James: In Raymond James's 2025 RIA benchmarking survey, respondents reported having a documented, long-term succession plan, a 40% improvement from 2023, and 87% reported having a catastrophic event plan. [RJF-PR-web-independent-ria-custody-services#1]
- C31 Schwab: On Schwab's 3Q25 earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025), the firm said it meets young investors in the channels where they seek information, noting they learn about finances primarily through social media and family. [SCHW-TR-2025Q3#11]
- C32 Schwab: On Schwab's 4Q25 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025), the firm said it opened 10 new branches and hired hundreds of financial consultants and wealth advisors in 2025. [SCHW-TR-2025Q4#4]
- C33 Schwab: On Schwab's 1Q25 earnings call (period ended March 31, 2025), the firm expected to grow roughly 250 new financial and wealth consultants during 2025, with continued investment in 2026 and 2027. [SCHW-TR-2025Q1#44]
- C34 Schwab: Schwab's 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states Schwab offers employee career-growth programs including mentorship, development, and leadership programs and education/training reimbursement. [SCHW-10K-2025#50]
- C35 Schwab: Trade press (August 20, 2026) reports Schwab is hiring thousands of financial consultants to add to its existing 3,000. [SCHW-TRADE-2026-08-20-RB-01#0]
- C36 Edward Jones: Edward Jones's 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) describes its comprehensive onboarding program for financial advisors, delivered through online self-paced modules, home office training or a virtual classroom, on-the-job training, examinations, financial planning learning, and in-region mentorship and coaching. [EDJ-10K-2025#29]
- C37 Edward Jones: Edward Jones's 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) says it is making significant investments in financial advisors, CSTPs and home office associates to strengthen the colleague experience and capabilities. [EDJ-10K-2025#70]
- C38 Merrill: Merrill's Advisor Development Program web page (page content as of November 3, 2026) says the program is designed for people interested in a wealth management career to help them identify client financial goals, align banking and investing solutions, and build a book of high-net-worth clients. [MER-PR-web-advisor-development-program#0]
- C39 Merrill: The same page states the program offers industry-leading training and dedicated study time for securities licenses, plus coaching and mentorship, and participants progress through three roles until graduating to Merrill Core Advisor. [MER-PR-web-advisor-development-program#1]
- C40 Merrill: The page states Path A (Advisor Development Program Client Associate) lasts approximately 13 months, including three months of licensing. [MER-PR-web-advisor-development-program#2]
- C41 Merrill: The page states the Advisor Development Program Financial Advisor role lasts approximately 1-4 years. [MER-PR-web-advisor-development-program#2]
- C42 Merrill: Merrill's becoming-an-advisor web page (page content as of November 3, 2026) states Merrill had 446 advisors named to the 2025 Forbes Top Next-Generation Wealth Advisors Best-in-State list, more than any other firm. [MER-PR-web-becoming-a-merrill-lynch-advisor#1]
- C43 Merrill: The page states that for the Forbes Top Next-Gen list, next-generation advisors are those born in 1986 or later with a minimum of four years as an advisor. [MER-PR-web-becoming-a-merrill-lynch-advisor#3]
- C44 Merrill: On Bank of America's 2Q25 earnings call (period ended June 30, 2025), management said Merrill continued to add financial advisors through its extensive training program and experienced hiring, and approximately one-third of net new households in Q2 2025 were added by newly trained advisors (covering Merrill and Bank of America Private Bank). [BAC-TR-2025Q2#34]
- C45 Raymond James: Raymond James's fiscal 2025 10-K (year ended September 30, 2025) states the firm offers the Wealth Management Associate Program for branch experience and the Advisor Mastery Program to begin building a client base for individuals who want to become financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#27]
- C46 Raymond James: Raymond James's 2025 investor day deck (published June 5, 2025) lists a Next gen advisor strategy among recruiting priorities. [RJF-DECK-2025-investor-day#42]
- C47 Raymond James: Raymond James's affiliation options web page (page content as of April 21, 2026) references New advisor training as part of its advisor offering. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#2]

- C49 Schwab: Schwab's 3Q26 press release (period ended September 30, 2026) reports it introduced Charley, an AI-powered assistant providing U.S. retail clients conversational access to Schwab's tools and insights. [SCHW-PR-2026-10-15-01#3]

- C69 This comparison is not valid on this evidence: the documents do not provide a common, directly comparable measure of the five firms' preparation for the generational wealth transfer. The preparation evidence is qualitative and firm-specific, with several firms silent on given aspects, and the only quantitative series (advisor headcount and net new assets) are defined differently across firms and reported over different periods, so the five firms cannot be ranked or compared on a shared basis. [MS-TR-2025Q2#65]

[MS-10K-2025#659]

### Q24 (gap_with_adjacent)

- C1 Across the five firms, client assets per advisor cannot be validly compared on this evidence: none of the five discloses a client-assets-per-advisor figure, and each firm defines the numerator and the advisor headcount on a different basis. Morgan Stanley's numerator is Wealth Management client assets, which it defines as assets for which Wealth Management provides services (MS-SUPP-2025Q4#35), and its headcount is Wealth Management representatives (MS-SUPP-2020Q4#1). Schwab's numerator is client assets, defined as the market value of all client assets in its custody (SCHW-10K-2025#118), and it reports no financial-advisor headcount, only Form ADV categories such as employees and registered representatives (SCHW-ADV1A-2026#1). Edward Jones reports client assets under care (EDJ-10Q-2026Q2#59) and financial advisors (EDJ-10K-2025#130). Merrill reports Merrill Wealth Management client balances (BAC-SUPP-2026Q3#53) but no Merrill-only advisor headcount: its wealth-advisor figure covers GWIM and Consumer Banking (BAC-SUPP-2023Q4#2), and its Form ADV figures are employees, broker-dealer registered representatives, and investment adviser representatives rather than financial advisors (MER-ADV1A-2026#1). Raymond James reports client assets under administration (RJF-PR-2025-10-22-01#27) and excludes RCS-division advisors from its financial advisor metric although their client assets are included in AUA (RJF-10K-2025#228; RJF-10K-2025#12). Raymond James itself states, "The definitions for client assets may be inconsistent across companies" (RJF-DECK-2025-investor-day#32). [MS-SUPP-2025Q4#35, MS-SUPP-2020Q4#1, SCHW-10K-2025#118, SCHW-ADV1A-2026#1, EDJ-10Q-2026Q2#59, EDJ-10K-2025#130, BAC-SUPP-2026Q3#53, BAC-SUPP-2023Q4#2, MER-ADV1A-2026#1, RJF-PR-2025-10-22-01#27, RJF-10K-2025#228, RJF-10K-2025#12, RJF-DECK-2025-investor-day#32]
- C2 Morgan Stanley: Client assets per financial advisor for each firm, expressed as total client assets divided by advisor headcount: not computed: the two figures cover different populations. Population per the MS 10-K 2025-12-31, dated December 31, 2025: "Wealth Management supports clients through three channels: Advisor-Led, Self-Directed and Workplace." Population per the MS supplement - Wealth Management segment 2020-12-31: "Advisor‐led client assets represents client assets in accounts that have a Wealth Management representative assigned." [MS-SUPP-2020Q4#1, MS-10K-2025#125, MS-SUPP-2020Q4#3]
- C3 Raymond James Financial: Client assets per financial advisor for each firm, expressed as total client assets divided by advisor headcount: not computed: the two figures cover different populations. Population per the RJF 10-K 2025-09-30: "Advisors in our RCS division are not included in our financial advisor metric although their client assets are included in PCG AUA." [RJF-10K-2025#227, RJF-PR-2025-10-22-01#9, RJF-10K-2025#228]

### Q25 (answer)

- C1 Morgan Stanley: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published November 4, 2026) states that a Morgan Stanley Virtual Advisor (MSVA) is paid a base salary plus a discretionary bonus and does not retain any portion of the Morgan Stanley Advisory Fee. [MS-ADV-2026#173]
- C2 Schwab: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that its compensation package includes base pay as a component, along with performance-based incentive pay, equity awards, recognition awards, and health and wellness benefits. [SCHW-10K-2025#50]
- C3 Edward Jones: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that financial advisors are generally compensated on a commission basis, subject to a minimum guaranteed salary, and may be entitled to additional compensation based on their respective branch office profitability and the profitability of the Partnership. [EDJ-10K-2025#30]
- C4 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that it pays Advisors a salary and incentive compensation based on the revenues Merrill receives for making available and/or providing Program Services and for brokerage services provided to clients with brokerage accounts. [MER-ADV-2026#402]
- C5 Merrill: Its Advisor Development Program page (page content as of November 3, 2026) lists base salary and unlimited earning potential among the offerings for advisors. [MER-PR-web-advisor-development-program#1]
- C6 Raymond James: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published September 14, 2026) states that newly affiliated RJA financial advisors through its Advisor Mastery Program may receive a salary in addition to advisory fees (or salary in addition to commissions and trails for brokerage accounts) for a period of time. [RJF-ADV-2026#50]
- C7 Raymond James: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published September 14, 2026) states that the salary paid by RJA declines each year as the number of clients the financial advisor serves and receives revenues in the Advisor Mastery Program increases. [RJF-ADV-2026#51]
- C8 Raymond James: The 2025 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states that its competitive pay packages include base salary, incentive bonus, and equity compensation programs. [RJF-10K-2025#29]
- C9 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) attributed higher Wealth Management compensation and benefits expenses primarily to an increase in the formulaic payout to Wealth Management advisors driven by higher compensable revenue. [MS-10K-2025#170]
- C10 Morgan Stanley: For Financial Advisors in the Select UMA Program, the 2026 Form ADV brochure (published November 4, 2026) states that the amount of the fee a client pays is a factor used in calculating the compensation paid to the Financial Advisor, and if the fee rate is below a certain threshold, the Financial Advisor is credited with less than the total fee for compensation purposes. [MS-ADV-2026#173]
- C11 Morgan Stanley: Trade press reports (InvestmentNews, published October 26, 2026) that Morgan Stanley is increasing a pay threshold for advisors by 10% in 2027, meaning advisors have to generate that much more revenue to see the same pay next year. [MS-TRADE-2026-10-26-IN-01#4]
- C12 Edward Jones: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published March 27, 2026) states that a client purchasing investments through Edward Jones as a broker-dealer would pay sales charges or commissions, a portion of which would be paid to the financial advisor, and that a financial advisor typically earns more in upfront fees and commissions with brokerage services but typically earns more over time with Advisory Solutions Fund Models. [EDJ-ADV-2026#65]
- C13 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that an Advisor is credited with 'production credits' from a portion of the Merrill Lynch Fee paid and, for brokerage accounts, the Sales Charges received from securities transactions, and that advisors also receive production credits for clients' margin lending, cash swept to Bank Affiliates, and referrals to BANA and other Merrill Affiliates. [MER-ADV-2026#402]
- C14 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that advisors receive 'production credits' toward their compensation formula based on the amount of the Merrill Lynch Fee that the client pays. [MER-ADV-2026#232]
- C15 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that its policies result in advisors receiving fewer production credits (by 10% or more) when the weighted average Merrill Lynch Fee Rate for a client's accounts and household is lower than the rate levels Merrill establishes for specific wealth asset tiers. [MER-ADV-2026#232]
- C16 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that the higher the Merrill Lynch Fee Rate a client agrees to, the more production credits the advisor earns, and the more overall production credits an advisor generates, the higher the advisor's incentive compensation. [MER-ADV-2026#404]
- C17 Raymond James: Its affiliation-options page (page content as of April 21, 2026) states that the Traditional Employee affiliation model offers up to 50% payout plus a full array of competitive employee benefits. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C18 Raymond James: Its affiliation-options page (page content as of April 21, 2026) states that the Advisor Select affiliation model offers a 70-80% gross payout plus a full array of competitive employee benefits. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C19 Raymond James: Its affiliation-options page (page content as of April 21, 2026) states that the Independent Contractor affiliation model offers an 81% to 90% payout. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#1]
- C20 Raymond James: Its affiliation-options page (page content as of April 21, 2026) states that the Corporate RIA affiliation model offers an 81% to 90% payout. [RJF-PR-web-affiliation-options#2]
- C21 Raymond James: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published September 14, 2026) states that, for financial advisors in advisory programs, depending on an advisor's annual revenue generation with the firm, the advisor can receive a higher portion of the fee paid to the firm, incentivizing higher payout percentages. [RJF-ADV-2026#50]
- C22 Raymond James: On the earnings call (period ended March 31, 2026; published April 22, 2026), management said that even on the employee side it has tiered payout systems, so as production increases an advisor moves higher up the payout grid. [RJF-TR-2026Q1#44]
- C23 Raymond James: On the earnings call (period ended March 31, 2026; published April 22, 2026), management said that independent advisor payouts are higher than employee advisor payouts because independent advisors cover their own overhead costs, real estate, and health insurance. [RJF-TR-2026Q1#44]
- C24 Raymond James: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published September 14, 2026) states that a financial advisor, as a registered representative, may receive additional compensation such as commissions and/or trail fees for providing brokerage transaction-related services. [RJF-ADV-2026#85]
- C25 Schwab: The January 21, 2026 press release (period ended December 31, 2025) states that it uses adjusted diluted EPS and return on tangible common equity as components of performance criteria for employee bonus and certain executive management incentive compensation arrangements, with the Compensation Committee maintaining discretion. [SCHW-PR-2026-01-21-01#56]
- C26 Edward Jones: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that if the Partnership's profitability decreases, bonuses paid to financial advisors may be decreased or eliminated, and that a significant portion of financial advisors' compensation is variable based on the Partnership's profitability. [EDJ-10K-2025#58]
- C27 Edward Jones: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published March 27, 2026) states that most financial advisors are eligible to participate in the Edward Jones Travel Award Program, which includes domestic and international travel or a cash award in lieu of a trip, and that eligibility is based on the amount of new and existing assets under care of the financial advisor. [EDJ-ADV-2026#67]
- C28 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that advisors are eligible to receive a compensation award, payable over a defined period of time, from an incentive program currently based on meeting prior-year growth targets in new households and in assets and liabilities. [MER-ADV-2026#406]
- C29 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that the growth in assets and liabilities component of the award is based on client asset movements into advisory programs, investment activity in specified products, new BANA trust accounts, 401(k) plan accounts, and clients' use of banking and lending services. [MER-ADV-2026#406]
- C30 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that advisors do not receive additional compensation as a result of advisory client assets held in the Cash Sweep Program. [MER-ADV-2026#406]
- C31 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that an advisor is subject to a reduction in their incentive compensation grid if they fail to achieve growth for two consecutive years in the assets and liabilities component of the award. [MER-ADV-2026#406]
- C32 Raymond James: The 2025 investor-day deck (published June 5, 2025) shows Incentive Compensation as 10% in its Private Client Group financial compensation and benefits breakdown. [RJF-DECK-2025-investor-day#92]
- C33 Raymond James: The 10-Q (period ended December 31, 2025; published February 6, 2026) states that the decrease in accrued compensation, commissions and benefits was primarily due to the annual payment in its fiscal first quarter of prior-year bonuses and certain benefits. [RJF-10Q-2025Q4#263]
- C34 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that deferred compensation for financial advisors in the Wealth Management business segment is generally composed of 75% cash-based awards and 25% stock-based awards, and the programs generally contain vesting, clawback and cancellation provisions. [MS-10K-2025#193]
- C35 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that employees can allocate deferred cash-based awards among a menu of notional investments selected by the Firm, and compensation expense is recognized over the vesting period relevant to each separately vesting portion of deferred awards. [MS-10K-2025#193]
- C36 Morgan Stanley: On the fourth-quarter 2025 earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025; published January 15, 2026), management said it will increase the cash component of advisor compensation in connection with transitioning DCP hedges to derivatives, to reduce accounting-driven volatility and simplify the compensation program. [MS-TR-2025Q4#22]
- C37 Morgan Stanley: The 2026 second-quarter 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2026) states that in the first quarter of 2026 it reduced the amount of deferred compensation as a proportion of total compensation for Wealth Management advisors. [MS-10Q-2026Q2#94]
- C38 Schwab: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that its deferred compensation plan for certain eligible employees permits participants to defer the receipt of certain cash compensation. [SCHW-10K-2025#495]
- C39 Schwab: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) reports a deferred compensation liability of $283 million at December 31, 2025. [SCHW-10K-2025#495]
- C40 Schwab: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) reports a deferred compensation liability of $251 million at December 31, 2024. [SCHW-10K-2025#495]
- C41 Raymond James: The 2025 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states that its LTIP is a non-qualified deferred compensation plan providing benefits to certain employees who meet certain compensation or production requirements, funded primarily by corporate-owned life insurance. [RJF-10K-2025#682]
- C42 Raymond James: The 2025 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states that for certain associates who meet compensation, production, or other criteria, it offers various non-qualified deferred compensation plans that provide a return and serve as a retention tool. [RJF-10K-2025#29]
- C43 Raymond James: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published September 14, 2026) states that qualification for recognition clubs can result in benefits to the financial advisor such as paid travel, reimbursement of certain business-related expenses, and deferred compensation awards. [RJF-ADV-2026#53]
- C44 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that certain current and former employees, including financial advisors in the Wealth Management segment, participate in the Firm's stock-based compensation plans, which include RSUs, PSUs and an ESPP. [MS-10K-2025#781]
- C45 Morgan Stanley: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that its Equity Incentive Compensation Plan permits awards consisting of stock options, stock appreciation rights, restricted stock, restricted stock units, performance-based units, and other equity-based or equity-related awards. [MS-10K-2025#890]
- C46 Schwab: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that its 2022 Stock Incentive Plan provides for granting stock options and restricted stock units to employees and non-employee directors. [SCHW-10K-2025#487]
- C47 Edward Jones: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published March 27, 2026) states that the Program Fee, assets under care, and client Reserve Line Advance balances may impact a financial advisor's eligibility for the receipt of certain limited partnership profits interest in The Jones Financial Companies, L.L.L.P. (the 'Profits Interest'). [EDJ-ADV-2026#67]
- C48 Raymond James: The 2025 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states it has one share-based compensation plan (the 2012 Stock Incentive Plan) for its employees, Board of Directors, and independent contractor financial advisors. [RJF-10K-2025#677]
- C49 Raymond James: The 2025 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states that its competitive pay packages include equity compensation programs, and that it may grant equity awards in connection with initial employment or under retention programs. [RJF-10K-2025#29]
- C50 Raymond James: The 2025 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states that its employee stock ownership plan (ESOP), for U.S.-based employees, held shares of common stock at September 30, 2025. [RJF-10K-2025#681]
- C51 Raymond James: The 2025 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states that the market value of its common stock held by the ESOP at September 30, 2025 was reported, a portion of which was unearned (not yet vested) by ESOP plan participants. [RJF-10K-2025#681]
- C52 Raymond James: The 2025 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states that under its 2003 Employee Stock Purchase Plan, the purchase price of the stock is 85% of the average high and low market price on the day prior to the purchase date. [RJF-10K-2025#681]
- C53 Raymond James: The 2025 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states it utilizes a trust, established and funded to acquire common stock in the open market, to settle RSUs granted as a retention vehicle for certain employees of its Canadian subsidiaries. [RJF-10K-2025#493]
- C54 Raymond James: Its Traditional Employee advisor page (page content as of May 16, 2026) states that its employee advisor experience is enabled by advisor book ownership, describing it as the advisor's own business, brand, and clients. [RJF-PR-web-traditional-employee-advisor#0]
- C55 Morgan Stanley: Trade press reports (InvestmentNews, published October 26, 2026) that Morgan Stanley is not introducing retention bonuses as part of its 2027 pay plan for advisors and is instead increasing a pay threshold for advisors by 10% in 2027. [MS-TRADE-2026-10-26-IN-01#4]
- C56 Morgan Stanley: Trade press reports (published November 10, 2026) that a FINRA arbitrator turned aside Morgan Stanley's attempt to claw back $2.46 million in recruiting bonus loans from an advisor, finding the notes had been paid in full before the hearing. [MS-TRADE-2026-11-10-IN-01#0]
- C57 Morgan Stanley: Trade press reports (published August 28, 2026) that Morgan Stanley sought to claw back recruiting bonus money from a former advisor in a dispute over promissory notes, loans that brokers pay back to firms over time, and a FINRA arbitrator awarded the firm $8.13 million in damages plus $561,000 in legal fees. [MS-TRADE-2026-08-28-IN-01#0]
- C58 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that advisors who join Merrill from other firms generally receive payments in connection with the move, which may take the form of salary guarantees, upfront awards, and compensation contingent on continued employment such as deferred payments and recruitment loans. [MER-ADV-2026#408]
- C59 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that the amount paid to advisors under these recruiting arrangements is generally based to a large extent on the size of the business at their prior firm. [MER-ADV-2026#408]
- C60 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that future recruiting payments based on assets that transfer are conditioned on the total assets in accounts the advisor services at Merrill, and these bonuses are in addition to the incentive compensation to which advisors are otherwise entitled. [MER-ADV-2026#408]
- C61 Raymond James: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published September 14, 2026) states that financial advisors are provided access to financial incentives for affiliating with the firm, including transition assistance, enhanced pay-outs, repayable business transition or working capital loans, and administrative fee reimbursements. [RJF-ADV-2026#54]
- C62 Raymond James: The January 28, 2026 press release (period ended December 31, 2025) states that PCG recruiting and retention-related compensation includes cash and equity awards issued for recruiting activities, as retention for existing advisors, or in connection with acquisitions, expensed over the requisite service period (typically 5 to 10 years). [RJF-PR-2026-01-28-01#71]
- C63 Raymond James: On the earnings call (period ended September 30, 2025; published October 22, 2025), management said that in fiscal year 2025, adjusted compensation expense included amortization of transition assistance to recruited advisors and retention awards to existing advisors totaling $355 million, up approximately 11% from fiscal 2024. [RJF-TR-2025Q3#20]
- C64 Raymond James: On the earnings call (period ended December 31, 2025; published January 28, 2026), management said that 100% of what it pays in transition assistance goes to retention rather than to a seller. [RJF-TR-2025Q4#44]
- C65 Raymond James: The 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2025) states it offers loans to prospective financial advisors for recruiting and retention purposes, with unfunded commitments of $15 million for loans to financial advisors who had met conditions as of June 30, 2025. [RJF-10Q-2025Q2#158]
- C66 Raymond James: The 10-Q (period ended June 30, 2026) states it offers loans to prospective financial advisors for recruiting and retention purposes, with unfunded commitments of $14 million for loans to financial advisors who had met conditions as of June 30, 2026. [RJF-10Q-2026Q2#167]
- C67 Raymond James: The 2025 10-K (fiscal year ended September 30, 2025) states it may incur costs in connection with loans or other incentives offered to newly recruited financial advisors and other key personnel. [RJF-10K-2025#89]
- A1 Schwab: The 2026 first-quarter 10-Q (period ended March 31, 2026) said compensation and benefits expense increased for both Investor Services and Advisor Services primarily due to annual merit increases, growth in headcount, higher incentive compensation, and higher other employee-related costs. [SCHW-10Q-2026Q1#60]
- A2 Edward Jones: The 2025 10-K (period ended December 31, 2025) states that each named executive officer participates in the Partnership's profit sharing and 401(k) plan, a qualified deferred compensation plan, with contributions based on the overall profitability of the Partnership. [EDJ-10K-2025#306]
- A3 Merrill: The 2026 Form ADV brochure (published October 30, 2026) states that it and its affiliates receive additional compensation ('Additional AI Compensation') under fee-sharing arrangements with managers of certain Alternative Investment Funds for their efforts related to distribution of interests in those funds. [MER-ADV-2026#436]
- A4 Schwab: The 2026 investor-day deck (published May 14, 2026) and the July 21, 2026 press release (period ended June 30, 2026) said it uses adjusted diluted EPS and return on tangible common equity as components of performance criteria for employee bonus and certain executive management incentive compensation arrangements. ### Q28 (answer)

- C1 Edward Jones: In its 10-Q for the period ended September 26, 2025 (published November 7, 2025), Edward Jones reported financial advisor attrition of 6.1% for the third quarter of 2025, defining attrition as the annualized number of financial advisors that left or retired from the Partnership during the period compared to the total number of financial advisors as of period end; it stated, "Financial advisor attrition was 6.1% as a result of a variety of factors, including a rise in financial advisor departures with tenure of five years or less." [EDJ-10Q-2025Q3#70, EDJ-10Q-2025Q3#69]
- C2 Edward Jones: In its 10-Q for the period ended June 27, 2025 (published August 8, 2025), Edward Jones reported financial advisor attrition of 6.4% for the second quarter of 2025, defining attrition as the annualized number of financial advisors that left or retired from the Partnership during the period compared to the total number of financial advisors as of period end; it stated, "Financial advisor attrition increased to 6.4% primarily due to an increase in retirements, consistent with industry trends." [EDJ-10Q-2025Q2#67, EDJ-10Q-2025Q2#66]
- C3 Edward Jones: In its 10-Q for the period ended September 26, 2025 (published November 7, 2025), Edward Jones reported financial advisor attrition of 4.7% for the third quarter of 2024, per its attrition percentage table. [EDJ-10Q-2025Q3#68, EDJ-10Q-2025Q3#69]
- C4 Edward Jones: In its 10-Q for the period ended September 26, 2025 (published November 7, 2025), Edward Jones reported financial advisor attrition of 6.0% for the nine months ended September 26, 2025, per its attrition percentage table. [EDJ-10Q-2025Q3#68, EDJ-10Q-2025Q3#69]
- C5 Edward Jones: In its 10-Q for the period ended September 26, 2025 (published November 7, 2025), Edward Jones reported financial advisor attrition of 5.1% for the nine months ended September 27, 2024, per its attrition percentage table. [EDJ-10Q-2025Q3#68, EDJ-10Q-2025Q3#69]
- C6 Merrill: On the second-quarter 2026 Bank of America earnings call (published July 14, 2026), CFO Alastair Borthwick said Merrill adviser attrition is now at near historic lows: "Well, adviser attrition is now at near historic lows. So we're pretty happy with that." [BAC-TR-2026Q2#63]
- C7 Edward Jones: In its 10-K for the period ended December 31, 2025 (published March 13, 2026), Edward Jones states it "experiences continued efforts by competing firms to hire away its financial advisors, although the Partnership believes its rate of attrition of financial advisors is in line with comparable firms." [EDJ-10K-2025#27]
- C8 Edward Jones: In its 10-Q for the period ended June 27, 2025 (published August 8, 2025), Edward Jones said the increase in its financial advisor attrition in the second quarter of 2025 was "primarily due to an increase in retirements, consistent with industry trends." [EDJ-10Q-2025Q2#67]
- C9 Edward Jones vs. Merrill: A numeric comparison of the two firms' financial advisor attrition rates with each other, or with an industry attrition rate, is not valid on this evidence. Edward Jones discloses a quantified, annualized attrition rate (6.1% for the third quarter of 2025 and 6.4% for the second quarter of 2025, under its definition of attrition as the annualized number of advisors who left or retired during the period compared to period-end advisor count), whereas Merrill's attrition is disclosed only qualitatively as being at "near historic lows" (Bank of America CFO, second-quarter 2026 earnings call), and the industry items measure a different quantity—advisors' stated intention to leave their employer within 12 months and advisors expected to retire in the next decade—not an industry attrition rate. Edward Jones's own comparison is limited to its belief that its rate of attrition is "in line with comparable firms." [EDJ-10Q-2025Q3#70, EDJ-10Q-2025Q3#69, EDJ-10Q-2025Q2#67, EDJ-10Q-2025Q2#66, BAC-TR-2026Q2#63, EDJ-10K-2025#27, KITCES-WELLBEING-2023#17, MCK-ADVISOR-SHORTAGE-2025#7]
- A1 Industry research: According to Kitces Research (published December 20, 2023), struggling advisors were 4X as likely as thriving advisors to be leaving their employer within the next 12 months; nearly one in 10 struggling advisors were extremely likely to be leaving their employer, compared to just 1 in 50 thriving advisors. [KITCES-WELLBEING-2023#17]
- A2 Industry research: McKinsey (published February 10, 2025) estimates that 110,000 advisors, 38 percent of the current total and representing 42 percent of total industry assets, are expected to retire in the next decade. [MCK-ADVISOR-SHORTAGE-2025#7]
